| L-30 | Commands CRUD (`commands/pages.rs`, `commands/tests/page_cmd_tests.rs`) — Subagent 1 | **`import_markdown_inner` aborts on first error (option b).** Replaced the per-block / per-property `match { Err => warnings.push }` with `?`-propagation. The `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` tx now rolls back on any per-row failure, restoring atomicity that the "log warnings, keep going" contract couldn't honour. `ImportResult { warnings: Vec<String>, ... }` shape preserved (parse-time depth-clamp warnings still surface on success); frontend's existing try/catch around `importMarkdown` already handles the new `Err` path. New `import_markdown_aborts_on_first_validation_error_l30` injects `priority:: 99` on block 2 of 3 (fails seeded select-options check) and asserts `Err` plus zero new rows in blocks/properties/op_log. |
| L-93 + L-94 | Search & Links (`tag_inheritance.rs`) — Subagent 2 | **`rebuild_all_split` consolidated into single-tx recursive-CTE INSERT.** New shape: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on `write_pool` → `DELETE FROM block_tag_inherited` → recursive-CTE `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO block_tag_inherited ... SELECT block_id, tag_id, inherited_from FROM descendant_tags` (same SQL shape as unified `rebuild_all`) → commit. Closes both L-93 (single-statement INSERT eliminates the per-row Rust-memory tuple set; chunking is moot) and L-94 (`apply_op_tag_inheritance` writers serialise behind the BEGIN IMMEDIATE — no read/DELETE race window). `read_pool` argument retained for `dispatch_split_or_single` API stability with a `let _ = read_pool;` and L-94 comment. 3 new tests: parity oracle vs unified, 600-block large-fixture parity, race-resolution via concurrent `tokio::spawn` of `AddTag` + rebuild asserting both pre-existing and concurrent tag survive. |
| L-108 | Lifecycle (`snapshot/tests.rs`) — orchestrator-direct | **Conflict-copy survives compaction test.** New `compact_then_apply_snapshot_preserves_conflict_copy_l108` builds page P + real block A + conflict copy A' (`is_conflict=1`, `conflict_source=A`) plus old `CreateBlock` op for A. `compact_op_log` purges A's old op and snapshots both blocks. Adds post-compaction noise B, reads snapshot blob, calls `apply_snapshot`. Asserts noise B is wiped (RESET), P+A+A' all present, A' still has `is_conflict=1` and `conflict_source=A`. Pure test addition. |
| L-109 | Lifecycle (`snapshot/tests.rs`) — orchestrator-direct | **Compaction atomicity on injected DELETE failure test.** New `compact_op_log_rolls_back_on_injected_delete_failure_l109` installs an `AFTER DELETE ON op_log` trigger that unconditionally `RAISE(ABORT)`s, runs `compact_op_log`, asserts (a) the call returns `Err`, (b) no `log_snapshots` row exists (the `INSERT … 'pending'` was rolled back; no orphaned 'pending' row leaks), (c) every `op_log` row is intact (the `DELETE` was rolled back). Pins the `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` whole-tx atomicity that a future split-tx refactor would silently break. The H-13 BEFORE-DELETE bypass mechanism is independent of this AFTER trigger, so the injection works even with the bypass active. |
| L-117 | MCP (no code change — entry was stale) — orchestrator-direct finding | **Already closed by prior H-2 work.** The REVIEW-LATER entry described `task_running` as "monotonic-true" because "the accept loop is an unconditional `loop { listener.accept().await? }`" with no shutdown branch. That changed when H-2 added the per-iteration `enabled` AtomicBool gate plus a `select!` arm in the accept loop that drops the listener and returns when `mcp_set_enabled(false)` clears the gate. After `serve` returns, `task_running.store(false)` runs in the spawn wrapper at `mcp/mod.rs:550-552` (and the RW twin at `mcp/mod.rs:643-645`). 3 H-2 acceptance tests in `mcp/server.rs` (`shutdown_closes_accept_loop_and_drops_listener` line 2686, `re_enable_rebinds_listener_after_shutdown` line 2763, `shutdown_during_active_connection_blocks_new_connects` line 2852) all use `wait_for_task_running_false(&lifecycle, ...)` to explicitly assert the flip-back-to-false invariant. Removed from REVIEW-LATER without code change — same pattern as session 529's L-73, session 525's L-35/L-134. |

**Process notes:**

- **PROMPT.md "don't wait idly" instruction honoured.** While 3 background subagents were processing L-30 / L-93+L-94 / L-13, the orchestrator picked up two small bounded test additions (L-108 + L-109) and a stale-entry investigation (L-117) — all completed before the subagents finished. The orchestrator's edits were stashed by parallel-agent contention once and recovered via `git stash pop`; both L-108 and L-109 tests survived intact, verified post-recovery via grep.
- **L-117's "already-closed" finding repeats the L-73 / L-35 pattern.** The REVIEW-LATER entry explicitly stated "automatically resolved by adding a shutdown signal that lets `serve` return" — H-2 was that shutdown signal, and 3 acceptance tests already lock in the `task_running` flip-back-to-false invariant. Like L-73 last batch, the entry aged in place after the underlying fix landed. Worth a periodic sweep of remaining LOW items for similar staleness — the pattern is now well-established (sessions 525, 529, 531 all had stale-entry findings).
- **L-13's "PERF-26 free fix" is the most valuable byproduct.** The investigation surfaced that `apply_remote_ops`'s bulk INSERT was missing `block_id` from its column list, so every sync-applied row had `op_log.block_id IS NULL`, defeating the migration-0030 index for the sync origin. Fixing this alongside L-13 was a 1-line addition (the sidecar is already populated by the validation-parse piggyback). Sync-side reads against the indexed column finally serve their query plan correctly.
- **L-93+L-94 paired refactor was the right shape.** L-94's option (c) (compute new tuple set + DELETE+INSERT in same write tx) automatically resolves L-93's perf concern because the INSERT becomes a single recursive-CTE `INSERT … SELECT` rather than per-row inserts. Both items closed by one cohesive change rather than two separate fixes. The unified `rebuild_all` shape is the oracle; the split version is now functionally equivalent (verified by the parity test). The split-pool pattern's "reduce writer-lock hold time" rationale is preserved — the new shape holds the writer for ONE tx with one DELETE + one INSERT…SELECT, much less than the old per-row INSERT loop.
- **L-30's "(b) abort on first error" was right despite the spec listing both options.** Savepoint nesting (option a) is invasive and error-prone; the existing "log warnings, keep going" contract was already broken (per the L-30 entry itself); the all-or-nothing path is honest and matches `?`-propagation patterns in `commands/blocks/crud.rs` and `commands/properties.rs`. Frontend impact is benign because `DataSettingsTab.tsx` already wraps `importMarkdown` in try/catch.
- **L-108's "noise B" assertion catches the right regression.** Beyond the conflict_source survival check, the test asserts that a post-compaction block (added between snapshot creation and `apply_snapshot`) is wiped by RESET. This pins the wipe-and-restore semantics across the round-trip — a future refactor that "merges" snapshot data with current state instead of replacing would silently break this.
- **L-109's AFTER DELETE trigger injection is the cleanest failure-injection mechanism for compaction.** The H-13 BEFORE DELETE trigger from migration 0036 is bypassed during compaction via `enable_op_log_mutation_bypass`, so a BEFORE trigger added in the test would be bypassed too. AFTER triggers fire after the DELETE attempt and are independent of the bypass mechanism, making them the right hook for "the DELETE itself succeeded but rollback must still work" tests. Documented this in the test comment for future maintainers.

## Session 530 — Backend LOW-severity parallel batch — close 5 items (L-90, L-92, L-110, L-111, L-114) (2026-04-28)

**5 backend LOW-severity REVIEW-LATER items closed in one PROMPT.md batch with 3 parallel build subagents + 1 orchestrator-direct fix.** All 3 subagents completed cleanly. The batch focuses on (1) Search & Links streaming-cap perf, (2) MCP forward-compat for multi-op tools, and (3) two test gaps (DST-safe recurrence + `apply_snapshot` chunk-rollback). End-of-batch review subagent verified the merged result.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**

- **LOW findings:** 28 → 23 (5 items resolved by code change).
- **Top-level open count (FEAT/MAINT/PERF/PUB/UX tracker):** 41 → 41.
- **Previously-resolved counter:** 681+ → 686+ across 497 sessions (latest session 530).

**Items closed (5):**

| Item | Subsystem / files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| L-90 | Search & Links (`link_metadata/mod.rs`, `link_metadata/tests.rs`) — Subagent 2 | **Streaming HTTP body cap.** `read_body_limited` now uses `response.chunk()` in a loop, accumulating into a `Vec<u8>` and stopping at `MAX_BODY_SIZE` (512 KiB). Optional `Content-Length` header is used as a `with_capacity` hint (clamped at the cap), strictly contract-compatible with the prior truncate-not-error semantics. Connection drops as soon as the cap is reached, preventing accidental OOM on a misbehaving server returning unbounded body. 2 new wiremock tests pin both behaviours: 1 MiB body → returned length ≤ MAX_BODY_SIZE; 100 KiB body → full round-trip. |
| L-92 | Search & Links (`fts/index.rs`, `fts/tests.rs`) — Subagent 2 | **Chunked tag-rename FTS reindex.** `reindex_fts_references` now iterates `unique_ids.chunks(FTS_REINDEX_CHUNK)` with a fresh `pool.begin()` per chunk (chunk size 1000). Pre-loads `(tag_names, page_titles)` once outside the loop. `pub(crate) const FTS_REINDEX_CHUNK: usize = 1000` exposes the boundary for tests. Renaming `#todo` referenced from 50K+ blocks no longer holds a single writer transaction for many seconds — chunks commit independently and a later-chunk failure leaves earlier chunks committed (acceptable because `fts_blocks` is an eventually-consistent cache; partial failure is recoverable on the next rebuild). New `reindex_fts_references_chunks_more_than_one_batch` test inserts `FTS_REINDEX_CHUNK + 50` (1050) referencing blocks via a single setup tx, renames the tag, and asserts `COUNT(*) = 1050` plus chunk-boundary samples (idx 0, 999, 1000, 1049) carry the new tag name. |
| L-110 | Lifecycle (`recurrence/tests.rs`) — orchestrator-direct | **DST-safe `shift_date_once` table-driven test.** New `shift_date_once_dst_transitions_calendar_safe` exercises 10 cases across known DST-transition dates in 2024–2025: Europe/London spring-forward (2024-03-31), London fall-back (2024-10-27), US Eastern spring-forward (2024-03-10), US Eastern fall-back (2024-11-03), Sydney spring-forward (2024-10-06), Sydney fall-back (2024-04-07), Lord Howe half-hour DST (2024-10-06), and US Eastern 2025 spring-forward stress. Pins the contract that calendar-day arithmetic on `NaiveDate` is timezone-independent, so a future refactor that introduces a `DateTime<Local>` (and thus a timezone-sensitive add) gets caught. Pure test addition; no production code change (Utc conversion deferred — recommendation noted that switching `Local::now()` → `Utc::now()` would change `.+`/`++` semantics). |
| L-111 | Lifecycle (`snapshot/tests.rs`) — Subagent 3 | **`apply_snapshot` chunk-rollback test.** New `apply_snapshot_rolls_back_chunk1_when_chunk2_fails` builds a `SnapshotData` whose `block_properties` has 166 valid rows in chunk-1 (= `MAX_SQL_PARAMS / 6`) and a chunk-2 with one fresh valid row plus a row whose `(block_id, key)` duplicates chunk-1 (violates `PRIMARY KEY`). Calls `apply_snapshot`, asserts `Err(AppError::Database(_))`, then asserts `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM block_properties = 0` (chunk-1's 166 rows must NOT be visible) AND `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM blocks = 0` (whole-tx rollback also drops the host block). The atomicity invariant holds today (single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` + `?`-propagation); a future refactor that splits the loop into multiple transactions would silently break it — this test catches it. Pure test addition. |
| L-114 | MCP (`task_locals.rs`, `mcp/activity.rs`, `mcp/server.rs`, `mcp/tools_ro.rs`, `mcp/tools_rw.rs`, `op_log.rs`) — Subagent 1 | **`LAST_APPEND` forward-compat for multi-op tools.** Storage migrated from `task_local!` `Cell<Option<OpRef>>` to `task_local!` `RefCell<Vec<OpRef>>`; `record_append` pushes onto the Vec instead of overwriting; new `take_appends() -> Vec<OpRef>` drains the storage (returns empty Vec outside scope, never panics). `ActivityEntry` and `ToolCompletionEvent` gained `additional_op_refs: Vec<OpRef>` with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]` for back-compat; `handle_tools_call` splits the drained Vec head/tail (head → `op_ref`, tail → `additional_op_refs`). Frontend `ActivityEntry` interface unchanged (the new field is optional and the frontend reads only `opRef`). Today every RW tool emits exactly one op; the type system now permits a future multi-op tool (e.g. `move_subtree`, `bulk_set_property`) to capture all op_refs without losing partial-Undo coverage. New tests: `record_append_retains_all_in_order`, `take_appends_drains_storage`, `activity_entry_serialises_additional_op_refs_when_present`, `handle_tools_call_multi_op_tool_splits_op_refs_head_and_tail`. Existing 4 task_local tests + 5 single-op MCP integration tests updated to use `take_appends()`. |

**Files touched (this session's batch — 12 modified):**

- Backend MCP forward-compat (6): `src-tauri/src/task_locals.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp/activity.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp/tools_ro.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp/tools_rw.rs`, `src-tauri/src/op_log.rs` (L-114)
- Backend Search+Links perf (4): `src-tauri/src/link_metadata/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/link_metadata/tests.rs` (L-90), `src-tauri/src/fts/index.rs`, `src-tauri/src/fts/tests.rs` (L-92)
- Backend Recurrence (1): `src-tauri/src/recurrence/tests.rs` (L-110)
- Backend Snapshot (1): `src-tauri/src/snapshot/tests.rs` (L-111)
- Docs (2): `REVIEW-LATER.md` (5 items removed; LOW count 28 → 23; previously-resolved counter bumped), `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry)

**Verification:** `prek run --all-files` → all 35 hooks PASS. Targeted runs:

- L-90: 73/73 `link_metadata` tests PASS including 2 new (truncates oversize / round-trips small).
- L-92: 126/126 `fts` tests PASS including new `reindex_fts_references_chunks_more_than_one_batch` (0.712s, 1050 rows across 2 chunks).
- L-110: 1/1 new DST table-driven test PASS in <0.01s; existing recurrence tests unchanged.
- L-111: 1/1 new `apply_snapshot_rolls_back_chunk1_when_chunk2_fails` PASS in 0.084s; 55/55 `snapshot::` tests PASS.
- L-114: 5/5 task_local + 241/241 MCP + 1/1 specta-bindings drift PASS; 18/18 activity tests PASS including new wire-shape contract test.

Full-suite post-merge: `cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast` → **3134/3134 passed**, 2 skipped. Frontend tests not exercised (zero frontend changes in this batch).

**Process notes:**

- **Parallel-agent contention re-emerged.** Subagent 1 (L-114) reported its first round of writes to `task_locals.rs` and `mcp/activity.rs` was silently stashed by another in-flight process via `git stash push tmp: unrelated WIP from other agents`. Subagent 1 detected the loss via `git status`, recovered via `git stash pop`, and re-applied identically. The orchestrator's L-110 test was also wiped during subagent execution and had to be re-applied. End-state was correct after re-application; full prek + 3134-test run confirmed cleanliness. **Lesson:** the orchestrator should re-verify its in-place edits AFTER all subagents complete, not just before. Adding this to the cancel-fallback playbook.
- **L-114's forward-compat shape preserves frontend types.** Two paths existed: (a) widen `ActivityEntry.op_ref: Option<OpRef>` → `Vec<OpRef>`, requiring a frontend type migration, or (b) keep `op_ref` as the head and add `additional_op_refs: Vec<OpRef>` as a new optional field. Subagent 1 chose (b) per the spec, which means: the frontend's hand-maintained `ActivityEntry` interface in `useMcpActivityFeed.ts` stays unchanged and silently ignores the new field. When the first multi-op tool ships, the frontend just needs to be taught to read `additionalOpRefs` and chain Undo across all of them — backend is ready today.
- **L-90 contract preservation was the right call over the spec's letter.** The recommendation suggested an early-error short-circuit on Content-Length > MAX_BODY_SIZE. The pre-existing function never errored on oversize input — it truncated. Subagent 2 preserved the truncate-not-error contract exactly and used Content-Length only as a `with_capacity` hint (clamped at the cap). This is strictly better: still benefits from the perf hint, doesn't change observable behaviour for any caller. The spec's "optionally" wording made this discretionary.
- **L-92's chunk-independent commit semantics are explicitly documented.** The function's rustdoc now states: "chunks commit independently for tag-rename FTS reindex; partial failure is recoverable since FTS is an eventually-consistent cache." This is a deliberate trade-off the recommendation called out. The previous single-tx semantics were wrong for the use case (rolling back a 50K-block reindex on the last row's failure was awkward) — chunked commits match the eventually-consistent cache model.
- **L-111's whole-tx rollback assertion catches the right regression.** The test asserts not just that `block_properties` chunk-1 rows are gone (the L-111 specific worry) but also that the host `blocks` row is gone. This second assertion catches a hypothetical refactor that splits the inserts across multiple transactions but keeps the host-block insert in the same tx. Both invariants are atomicity-of-the-snapshot-restore-as-a-whole, which is the user-visible contract — it's correct to pin both.
- **L-110 deferred the parser switch to UTC.** The recommendation had two paths: (1) add a DST test, (2) switch `Local::now()` → `Utc::now()` in the parser to remove timezone dependency. Path 2 is a behaviour change for users in non-UTC zones (`.+` semantics shift by ≤1 day) and would need user approval per Architectural Stability. Path 1 is contract-preserving and catches the calendar-arithmetic regressions the entry was actually about. Going with path 1 only; path 2 left as a future MAINT item if needed.

- L-61: 210/210 sync tests PASS including new `daemon_branch_b_dispatches_all_peers_in_round_l61` smoke test.
- L-73: 0 new code changes; 2 pre-existing tests (`apply_remote_ops_detects_fork_with_same_seq_different_hash` + `_does_not_count_real_duplicates_as_forks`) confirmed in place.
- L-106: doc-only, no test surface.
- L-124: 1/1 stress test (`concurrent_rw_clients_serialize_correctly_l124`) PASS in 0.289s — verifies BEGIN IMMEDIATE serialization holds at the MCP boundary under 4 agents × 6 iters interleaved with a frontend writer.
- L-135: 84/84 draft + recovery tests PASS including 3 new sweep-orphan tests.

Full-suite post-merge: `cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast` → **3126/3126 passed**, 2 skipped. Frontend tests not exercised (zero frontend changes in this batch).

**Process notes:**

- **L-73's "already closed" finding is the most valuable byproduct of the subagent process.** Subagent 3 read the existing `apply_remote_ops` code before adding its planned test, discovered the H-14 fix had already landed (with two locking tests at `sync_protocol/tests.rs:3637+`), and reported the REVIEW-LATER entry as stale rather than blindly adding a third redundant test. Removing the stale entry without code change is the right hygiene — it shrinks the LOW backlog by reflecting reality. This is the second time in recent sessions (after session 525's L-35/L-134) that a REVIEW-LATER item turned out to be already-resolved by prior work; the pattern is that grep-driven REVIEW-LATER analyses age in place. Worth a periodic sweep.
- **L-61's `JoinSet::abort_all()` for cancel-handling is the right shape.** The original sequential `for ... break` pattern only worked because the next peer wasn't yet dispatched. Concurrent dispatch means all peers are already in flight when one reports `was_cancelled`; aborting the still-running tasks is the equivalent semantics. The M-46 break-contract test (Branch C) continues to pass because Branch C still uses the sequential pattern — the L-61 change is scoped to Branch B only, as the spec required.
- **L-48 wrapper sanitization left some wrappers untouched intentionally.** Three wrappers (`mcp_disconnect_all`, `force_gcal_resync`, `log_frontend`) have `Ok(())`-only bodies — sanitize would be a mathematical no-op. Adding `.map_err(sanitize_internal_error)` to a `Result<(), Infallible>` would be visible noise, not a meaningful guard. Subagent 1 documented this explicitly per-wrapper.
- **L-124's stress test exercises the BEGIN IMMEDIATE serialization at the MCP boundary.** The original RO concurrent-clients test only proved RO tools serialize reads correctly (which is trivially true for SQLite); the L-124 RW variant proves writes serialize correctly across MCP agent connections AND a concurrent frontend writer. The four assertions (block count, tag inheritance, FK, no duplicate seqs) catch four distinct regression shapes. Worth keeping as a regression net for any future MCP-side caching layer that might subvert `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.
- **L-135's periodic wiring left as a follow-up.** The recommendation has two halves: (1) expose `sweep_orphan_drafts` as a function, (2) wire it into a periodic task. Subagent 2 delivered (1) and documented (2) as out-of-scope. The function being available is the load-bearing fix because boot recovery now also calls it implicitly via the warn+delete path; periodic-mid-session sweeps are nice-to-have but not load-bearing.

## Session 528 — Backend LOW-severity parallel batch — close 7 items (L-24, L-28, L-103, L-112, L-121, L-125, L-127) (2026-04-28)

**7 backend LOW-severity REVIEW-LATER items closed in one PROMPT.md batch with 3 parallel build subagents + 2 orchestrator-direct fixes + cancel-fallback for the third subagent.** 3 subagents launched in parallel; one (MCP normalization + merge logging) was canceled by the user mid-flight, and the orchestrator picked up its 2 items (L-121 + L-112) directly per the session-523 cancel-fallback pattern. End-of-batch review subagent verified the merged result.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**

- **LOW findings:** 42 → 35 (7 items resolved by code change).
- **Top-level open count (FEAT/MAINT/PERF/PUB/UX tracker):** 41 → 41.
- **Previously-resolved counter:** 667+ → 674+ across 495 sessions (latest session 528).

**Items closed (7):**

| Item | Subsystem / files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| L-24 | Cache+Pagination (`cache/block_links.rs`, `cache/tests.rs`) — Subagent 1 | **Chunked DELETE/INSERT via `json_each`.** `reindex_block_links` and `reindex_block_links_split` previously did 2N round-trips per re-index (per-target DELETE + per-target INSERT OR IGNORE). Now both impls do exactly 2 round-trips: `DELETE FROM block_links WHERE source_id = ? AND target_id IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?))` and `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO block_links (source_id, target_id) SELECT ?, value FROM json_each(?) WHERE EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM blocks WHERE id = value)` — preserves the EXISTS filter so dangling targets don't FK-violate. 4 new tests cover removals (50→0), additions (0→50), mixed diff (15-overlap), and a 600-link stress past the SQLite 999-bind ceiling. |
| L-28 | Cache+Pagination (`cache/tests.rs`) — orchestrator-direct | **CTE oracle test.** New `rebuild_page_ids_oracle_matches_rust_ancestor_walk` builds a synthetic vault (3 page roots, deeply-nested non-conflict blocks, 2 conflict copies, 1 orphan), computes the expected `page_id` per block via a Rust HashMap walk that mirrors the SQL CTE's documented semantics (climb until page or conflict, depth-bounded at 100), runs the production rebuild, and asserts byte-identical results. Closes the L-28 regression-net gap for `rebuild_page_ids`'s recursive ancestor walk — invariant #9 (`is_conflict = 0` on both CTE members) now has a Rust-side oracle. Spot-checks the deeply-nested, orphan, and conflict shapes. |
| L-103 | Recovery (`recovery/boot.rs`, `recovery/tests.rs`) — orchestrator-direct | **Once-per-process guard.** New `static RECOVERY_DONE: AtomicBool` enforces the documented "exactly once at start-up" contract. `compare_exchange(false, true, AcqRel, Acquire)` flips the flag atomically on the first call; subsequent calls return `AppError::InvalidOperation` referencing L-103 BEFORE touching the pool. Test-only `reset_recovery_guard()` (cfg-gated) + `recover_at_boot_test` wrapper let existing tests run their own recoveries against fresh pools. New `recover_at_boot_returns_err_on_second_call_without_reset` calls the production function directly to assert the guard fires. 26 existing test callsites mechanically updated to use the wrapper; the `recovery_idempotency_second_run_is_noop` test still passes (the wrapper resets between calls, exercising the data idempotency separately from the L-103 guard). |
| L-112 | Merge (`merge/detect.rs`) — orchestrator-direct (cancel-fallback) | **Conflict telemetry.** `merge_text` now emits per-branch `tracing::info!` lines: clean merges log `clean = true`; conflicts log `clean = false`, `conflict_len = <bytes>`, and `conflict_digest = <16-char blake3 prefix>`. The digest is a stable fingerprint for the kind of conflict observed, derived from diffy's `<<<<<<< / >>>>>>>` marker payload — useful for telemetry / bug-report triage / regression tests that need to pin the kind of conflict without leaking the underlying user text. Behaviour preserved (still returns `MergeResult::Conflict { ours, theirs, ancestor }`). |
| L-121 | MCP (`mcp/handler_utils.rs`, `mcp/tools_ro.rs`, `mcp/tools_rw.rs`) — orchestrator-direct (cancel-fallback) | **ULID normalization at the MCP boundary.** New `normalize_ulid_arg` helper in `handler_utils.rs` uppercases length-26 ASCII-alphanumeric inputs (the canonical Crockford-base32 ULID shape), passing other inputs through unchanged so downstream `*_inner` validation still produces the right error. Applied to every `*_id` field in MCP tool handlers: `block_id` / `parent_id` / `tag_id` / `value_ref` / `page_id` across `handle_get_page` / `handle_search` / `handle_get_block` / `handle_list_backlinks` / `handle_append_block` / `handle_update_block_content` / `handle_set_property` / `handle_add_tag` / `handle_delete_block`. SQLite's default `=` is case-sensitive, so a lowercase ULID returned `NotFound` before; now the agent can supply any case. 3 unit tests pin the helper contract (uppercases ULID-shape, passes non-ULID through, mixed case → uppercase) plus 1 MCP-level integration test (`get_block_accepts_lowercase_ulid_l121`). |
| L-125 | GCal (`gcal_push/api.rs`) — Subagent 2 | **`InstantBucket::take()` releases lock during sleep.** Refactored from `async fn take(&mut self)` to `async fn take(bucket: &Mutex<Self>)`. The function now acquires the guard each iteration, computes `sleep_for` while holding it, calls `drop(guard)` explicitly, sleeps, re-acquires, and pushes. Concurrent callers can overlap when the bucket isn't saturated — the previous "lock held across sleep" behaviour fully serialised them. Doc-comments updated; the misleading "we are already inside the caller's `.lock().await` guard" comment is gone. New `instant_bucket_releases_lock_during_sleep` test asserts the lock is free during sleep (would fail under the pre-fix impl). |
| L-127 | GCal (`gcal_push/mod.rs`) — Subagent 2 | **Visibility narrowed: `pub mod` → `pub(crate) mod`.** All 8 submodules (`api`, `connector`, `digest`, `dirty_producer`, `keyring_store`, `lease`, `models`, `oauth`) downgraded. No targeted re-exports needed — every external reference uses `crate::gcal_push::<submodule>::<symbol>` paths and stays valid with `pub(crate)`. The narrowing exposed 33 pre-existing `dead_code` warnings (mostly in `oauth.rs` — heavily test-tested but no production caller — plus `connector::FixedClock` test shim, `lease::with_backend`, `models::list_event_map_dates`, `api::get_event`). Per-submodule `#[allow(dead_code)]` attributes added to `api`, `connector`, `keyring_store`, `lease`, `models`, `oauth` (all 6 affected submodules) with a doc-block explaining the L-127 visibility narrowing exposed the warnings. Module-level docs name the future audit as out-of-scope (either wire the OAuth helpers into a production caller or `#[cfg(test)]`-gate the test-only shims). |

**Files touched (this session's batch — 16 modified, 2 new sqlx caches not needed):**

- Backend Core (1): `src-tauri/src/op_log.rs` (no L-103 change here, but earlier L-5 edits coexist cleanly)
- Backend Cache+Pagination (2): `src-tauri/src/cache/block_links.rs`, `src-tauri/src/cache/tests.rs`

- L-28: 1/1 oracle test (`rebuild_page_ids_oracle_matches_rust_ancestor_walk`) PASS — covers conflict copies, deep nesting, orphans.

**Process notes:**

- **Subagent 3 cancellation handled per session-523 pattern.** The user canceled the MCP normalization + merge logging subagent mid-flight. Working tree was empty (no partial state to merge or revert), so the orchestrator picked up L-121 + L-112 directly. Both items are well-scoped (L-121 is mechanical normalization, L-112 is single-callsite logging). Pattern is now well-established across sessions 520, 523, 528.
- **L-127 visibility narrowing surfaces pre-existing dead code as a feature.** The pub→pub(crate) move exposed 33 dead-code warnings — all in `oauth.rs`, `connector.rs`, `lease.rs`, `models.rs`, `api.rs`, `keyring_store.rs`. None of these are L-127 regressions; they're items previously masked by Rust's "skip dead-code analysis on `pub` items" heuristic. Per-submodule `#[allow(dead_code)]` is the right tool here — narrower than module-level `#![allow(...)]`, broader than per-item attrs (33 of them would be unwieldy). The genuinely-test-only items (`FixedClock`, `with_backend`) and the OAuth integration stubs (`begin_authorize`, `exchange_code`, `refresh_token`, `fetch_with_auto_refresh`) are now visible to a future audit. Documented as out-of-scope follow-up in the mod.rs doc-block.

Added the missing `None` `caller_context` arg to all 3 callsites. This kind of "tests but not benches" miss is exactly the trap session 526's process-notes warned about — `--tests` doesn't include `--all-targets`.
- **L-103 test-wrapper pattern is broadly reusable.** The `static AtomicBool` + `reset_*` test helper + `*_test` wrapper pattern is the cleanest way to wrap a once-per-process invariant for a test suite where each `tokio::test` shares the process under `cargo test` (but not under `cargo nextest`). The wrapper's `super::boot::reset_recovery_guard()` is `#[cfg(test)]`-gated so production code can't accidentally call it. Pattern is reusable wherever a similar guard is needed.
- **L-28 oracle test is the canonical reusable shape for AGENTS.md "Pattern #8".** Builds a synthetic vault, computes expected via a hand-rolled reference implementation, runs the production query, and compares row sets. Spot-checks the most important shapes to catch the "vacuously-true oracle" bug. Cheap, regression-resilient, and self-documenting via the test name. Should be applied to the next recursive CTE that gets refactored.

Sister `fetch_descendants_of` for L-84 (different SQL shape) reused the same constant. Future contributors landing a fourth IN-clause backlink site have one copy to reuse and one constant to compare against. The exclusion-only branch in `filters.rs` keeps an inline switch because its CTE is nested inside `NOT IN` — flagged by the subagent as a minor follow-up refactor opportunity (left as-is for this batch).

The subagent placed the lex-monotonic-Z-suffix invariant on `OpRecord::created_at` instead (Rust-side, where the contract is enforced). This is the right place — the migration SQL can't express the stricter invariant anyway.

End-of-batch review subagent ran across all six and confirmed PASS with no fix-ups.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**

- **Top-level open count (FEAT/MAINT/PERF/PUB/UX tracker):** 41 → 41.

**Items closed (6):**

| Item | Subsystem / files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| L-21 | Cache+Pagination (`pagination/history.rs`) | **Doc-only.** New `# Cursor seq invariant (L-21)` doc-block on `list_block_history` names the `op_log.seq ≥ 1` assumption explicitly, points at the `COALESCE(MAX(seq), 0) + 1` computation in `op_log::append_local_op_in_tx` as the source of truth, and tells the next maintainer what to do if a future change introduces a per-device seq-0 sentinel (switch `cursor_seq` to `Option<i64>` and bind directly). Inline comment at the cursor unpack also cross-references the doc-block so the `unwrap_or(0)` sentinel reads as deliberate, not accidental. |
| L-22 | Cache+Pagination (`pagination/history.rs`) | **Compile-time SQL check.** The `list_page_history` `__all__` branch was using `sqlx::query_as::<_, HistoryEntry>(…)` + `.bind()` — bypassing AGENTS.md invariant #6 (compile-time SQL validation). Converted to `sqlx::query_as!`; the `?N IS NULL` short-circuits and `IN (SELECT …)` subquery work fine inside the macro. New `.sqlx/query-32f35073…9d9092.json` cache entry committed. The page-scoped branch already used `query_as!`, so both branches now have the same compile-time guarantee. |
| L-27 | Cache+Pagination (`cache/agenda.rs`, `cache/tests.rs`) | **De-dup.** The 51-line UNION ALL desired-state SQL was duplicated verbatim in `rebuild_agenda_cache_impl` (single-pool) and `rebuild_agenda_cache_split_impl` (read/write-split). Extracted to `const DESIRED_AGENDA_SQL: &str` at the top of `agenda.rs` with a doc-block enumerating the four sources, the template-page `NOT EXISTS` filter, the conflict-aware invariant, and the deduplication semantics. Both impls now bind the const. New parity oracle test `agenda_rebuild_single_and_split_produce_identical_cache` runs both impls against a 4-source fixture (date-property, date-tag, due_date column, scheduled_date column, plus an excluded soft-deleted row) and asserts `agenda_cache` row sets are byte-identical via `BTreeSet` comparison. The test fails loudly if a future contributor accidentally edits one branch without the other. |
| L-31 | Commands/CRUD (`commands/history.rs`) | **Doc-only.** `restore_page_to_op_inner` reads `ops_after` against the bare pool; the downstream `revert_ops_inner` opens its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. New ops landing between read and write are not reverted — the function's contract is "snapshot-at-read-time", not "everything ever". New `# Snapshot semantics (L-31)` doc-block names the behaviour explicitly, anchors it in the single-user threat model (the practical window is one DB round-trip), and tells the next maintainer that lifting the read into the write tx would close the window but is not motivated today. |
| L-66 | Sync (`sync_daemon/snapshot_transfer.rs`, `sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs`, `sync_protocol/orchestrator.rs`) | **Defence-in-depth + new tests.** `try_receive_snapshot_catchup` previously gated the `peer_refs` upsert on `if !remote_device_id.is_empty()` — when HeadExchange carried only our own heads, the snapshot was applied but the peer was forgotten and the next sync started from scratch. New parameter `expected_remote_id: Option<&str>` mirrors the SyncComplete fallback in `SyncOrchestrator`: prefer session-level `remote_device_id`, fall back to `expected_remote_id` (mTLS / mDNS peer identity, exposed via a new `SyncOrchestrator::expected_remote_id()` accessor), or return `AppError::InvalidOperation` so the scheduler records the failure. The error message embeds "L-66" for traceability. Three new tests: fallback path resolves to expected_remote_id (with `peer_refs::get_peer_ref(&pool, "")` asserted to return `None`), both-empty path errors out and the message contains "L-66", and session-id-wins-over-expected when both are present. Updated 7 callsites (1 production caller in `sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs` + 5 existing tests in `snapshot_transfer.rs` + 1 in `sync_daemon/tests.rs`) to pass the new parameter — `None` for tests since they don't simulate the empty-HeadExchange edge case. |
| L-71 | Sync (`sync_daemon/server.rs`, `sync_protocol/orchestrator.rs`) | **Defence-in-depth wiring.** Responder's `SyncOrchestrator` was constructed without `with_expected_remote_id(...)`, so the orchestrator's HeadExchange-vs-expected mismatch path (covered by `orchestrator_rejects_unexpected_peer_device_id` at the unit level) never fired on the responder side. Restructured `handle_incoming_sync` to defer orchestrator construction until *after* the cert verification block, then wire the verified TLS certificate CN (`conn.peer_cert_cn()`) as `expected_remote_id`. The cert CN is already verified against the HeadExchange `device_id` by `verify_peer_cert`, so this is genuinely redundant defence-in-depth — but it activates the orchestrator's parallel mismatch check at zero risk. In-memory test connections (no peer cert) skip the wiring; existing 10 `handle_incoming_sync` tests continue to pass unchanged. |

- L-21 / L-22: `cargo nextest run` for `list_block_history` / `list_page_history` paths PASS; sqlx compile-time check now covers the `__all__` branch.
- L-27: 6/6 agenda-cache tests pass including the new parity oracle.

Full-suite post-merge: `cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast` → **3091/3091 passed**, 2 skipped. Frontend tests not exercised (zero frontend changes in this batch).

**Process notes:**

- **6-item orchestrator-direct batch is the right size for this opportunistic mode.** Session 524 ran 3 items; this one ran 6. The cap is "items that are individually understandable and can be reviewed in one pass at the end." Any larger and the end-of-batch review subagent starts losing context; any smaller and the per-item overhead (REVIEW-LATER edits, SESSION-LOG entry, prek run) dominates the actual fix work. Pattern: pick a batch where the orchestrator can hold all items in working memory, do them sequentially, and run a single review subagent at the end.
- **Sync stack defence-in-depth pairs well as a unit.** L-66 (peer_refs fallback) and L-71 (cert CN → expected_remote_id) are independent fixes but exercise adjacent abstractions in the same file family. Pairing them in one batch made it natural to reason about the SyncOrchestrator's `expected_remote_id` field as the unifying mechanism — the new `SyncOrchestrator::expected_remote_id()` accessor introduced for L-66 is exactly the same one L-71 would have needed had it landed independently. Co-located fixes in the same subsystem amortise the cognitive load.
- **The L-27 parity-oracle pattern is broadly reusable.** When two implementations bind the same logical query against different pool wirings (single-pool vs read/write-split), an oracle test that runs both against the same fixture and asserts `BTreeSet` equality is far cheaper than maintaining two parallel test suites. Same shape would work for any future read/write-split additions to the cache layer.
- **Deferring orchestrator construction in server.rs (L-71) was the cleanest fix.** The original code constructed `orch` on line ~84, then read the cert CN inside the if-let block ~80 lines later. Moving construction below the cert check let us conditionally wire `with_expected_remote_id(cert_cn)` without a setter. Existing tests cover both the cert-present and no-cert (in-memory) paths, so the restructuring is verified by the unchanged test surface.

## Session 524 — Backend LOW-severity opportunistic batch — close 3 items (L-23, L-115, L-120) (2026-04-28)

**3 backend LOW-severity REVIEW-LATER items closed as an opportunistic between-batch fill.** Single-orchestrator flow; no subagents. The user flagged "main agent is free, pick a new small fix and do it, don't wait idly", so the batch deliberately stayed tight: one validation tightening, one test brittleness fix, one doc-only clarification. All three are MCP / pagination boundary items in spirit even though they live in different files.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**

- **LOW findings:** 70 → 67 (3 items resolved by code change).
- **Top-level open count (FEAT/MAINT/PERF/PUB/UX tracker):** 41 → 41.
- **Previously-resolved counter:** 639+ → 642+ across 491 sessions (latest session 524).

**Items closed (3):**

| Item | Subsystem / files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| L-23 | Cache+Pagination (`pagination/properties.rs`) | **Boundary tightening.** `query_by_property` historically applied two different precedence rules to `value_text` + `value_date` depending on routing branch: reserved-column path (`due_date`, `scheduled_date`) silently picked `value_date.or(value_text)`, while non-reserved path AND-intersected both filters in SQL — almost always returning empty because a `block_properties` row stores its value in exactly one column. The fix rejects the conflict at the boundary with `AppError::Validation`, eliminating the silent-precedence bug shape. Doc-comment reorganised under a "Value filter (L-23)" section that names both historical behaviours. New test `query_by_property_rejects_both_value_filters` exercises both routing branches (non-reserved key `todo`, reserved-column key `due_date`) to pin the contract uniformly. |
| L-115 | MCP (`mcp/tools_ro.rs`) | **Test brittleness fix.** `list_property_defs_happy_path` previously asserted `arr.len() == 19` against the seeded property defs — every migration that adds or removes a seeded def would surface as a "MCP" failure even though the actual change is in migrations. Replaced with (a) a stable subset check for the four reserved-column keys (`todo_state`, `priority`, `due_date`, `scheduled_date`) per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability — properties system extension point", and (b) a meta-cross-check that `arr.len()` matches `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM property_definitions` against the live DB so drift in either direction (added / removed seeds) is detected without a hand-edited count constant. The insta snapshot remains the wire-shape oracle. New `.sqlx/` cache entry committed for the count query. |
| L-120 | MCP (`mcp/mod.rs`) | **Doc-only.** `McpLifecycle::disconnect_all` doc-block now explicitly names the edge-triggered semantics of `Notify::notify_waiters` — only tasks already inside `Notify::notified().await` are woken; connections that arrive *after* the call register a fresh waiter on entry to `run_connection` and observe no permit. Cross-references `shutdown` for the steady-state listener-stop pairing. The behavioural contract is unchanged; this is the documented-but-not-discoverable Tokio trap that the original "wakes every in-flight connection" line obscured. |

- Backend Cache+Pagination (1): `src-tauri/src/pagination/properties.rs`
- Backend Pagination tests (1): `src-tauri/src/pagination/tests.rs`
- Backend MCP (2): `src-tauri/src/mcp/tools_ro.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp/mod.rs`
- Backend sqlx cache (1 new): `src-tauri/.sqlx/query-63e750c2…fe9aa.json` (`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM property_definitions`)
- Docs (2): `REVIEW-LATER.md` (3 items removed; LOW count 70 → 67; previously-resolved counter bumped), `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry)

**Process notes:**

- **Opportunistic between-batch fill.** Recent sessions (522, 523) ran 6-7-item PROMPT.md batches; this session was an explicit "main agent is free" tap-in for a smaller, tighter set. The structural discipline still applies (test-per-change, doc-update, REVIEW-LATER hygiene) but the orchestration is leaner — no subagents, no worktrees, no review pipelining. Pattern is suitable for any 2-3 item LOW batch where the items are file-disjoint and individually <30 LOC.
- **`SELECT COUNT(*)` cross-check is reusable.** The L-115 pattern (assert response count matches live `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM <table>`) is the canonical way to keep insta-snapshot tests honest against migration drift — it adds one round-trip but turns "failed because the count is wrong" into "failed because reality drifted from the snapshot, here's exactly which way". Cheap. Worth adopting wherever a snapshot pins row count today.
- **Edge-triggered Notify is a documented-but-not-discoverable trap.** `Notify::notify_waiters` only wakes tasks already parked inside `Notify::notified().await`. The contract is in the Tokio docs but easy to miss when reading "kill switch" code. The L-120 fix doesn't change behaviour; it makes the trap visible to the next contributor.
- **L-23 motivates a future API simplification.** With both branches now uniform, the next refactor opportunity is to collapse `value_text: Option<&str>` + `value_date: Option<&str>` into a single `value: Option<PropertyFilterValue>` enum (`Text(&str)` / `Date(&str)`) — making the "exactly one or none" invariant a type-level property instead of a runtime check. Filed implicitly as a future refactor; not added to REVIEW-LATER because the current shape now behaves correctly.

## Session 523 — Backend LOW-severity cleanup batch — close 6 items (L-29, L-35, L-51, L-52, L-128, L-134) (2026-04-28)

**6 backend LOW-severity REVIEW-LATER items closed in one PROMPT.md batch.** Mixed orchestrator-direct + subagent flow. Both build subagents (L-35+L-134 in `commands/spaces.rs` and L-52 in `commands/bug_report.rs`) were canceled by the user mid-flight; the orchestrator picked up both directly. **Two items (L-35 + L-134) were discovered to be already resolved** by an earlier session's MAINT-112 `CommandTx` migration — `dispatch_background_for_page_create` no longer exists, op records are now threaded through `tx.enqueue_background(...)` and dispatched via `tx.commit_and_dispatch(materializer)`. **Process notes:**

- **Both build subagents canceled mid-flight; orchestrator-direct fallback worked cleanly.** The L-35+L-134 subagent and the L-52 subagent were both canceled by the user. Worktrees were empty (no partial state to merge or revert), so the orchestrator picked up both items directly in the main tree. Pattern is now well-established (session 520's MCP subagent had the same cancel-fallback flow): if a subagent stalls, orchestrator-direct is a sound finish.
- **Two items already resolved by prior session's refactor.** L-35 and L-134 both pointed at `dispatch_background_for_page_create` — a function that no longer exists. MAINT-112's `CommandTx` migration eliminated the re-fetch helper entirely; `tx.enqueue_background(record)` during the transaction + `tx.commit_and_dispatch(materializer)` after commit makes the dispatch structurally inseparable from the commit. Both items removed from REVIEW-LATER without code changes — the cleanup is part of normal review hygiene.
- **L-128 tripwire test pattern is reusable.** The pattern (a `#[test]` that asserts a constant ≤ a heuristic ceiling, with a clear failure message naming the optimisation that must precede a higher value) is a clean way to "freeze" performance assumptions. The orchestrator-style note is: if the tripwire ever fires, the contributor MUST either (a) implement the optimisation OR (b) update the heuristic ceiling with explicit rationale — not just bump the constant. The `#[allow(clippy::assertions_on_constants)]` is necessary because clippy correctly observes both sides are compile-time constants; collapsing to `const { assert!(...) }` would convert the tripwire to a build error, which is more aggressive than intended.
- **L-29's `unreachable!()` → `Err(Validation)` is a small but consequential fix.** Adding a fifth reserved column (e.g., `effort` per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability — properties system extension point") will now produce a clean error message at the IPC boundary if the contributor forgets to update the routing match. Previously it would have panicked in production. The doc-comment names this trap explicitly.
- **L-51 bindings.ts regen produces some whitespace noise.** The L-51 doc-comment update is the only semantic change; specta's emitter also added trailing spaces to many unrelated doc-continuation lines + a trailing newline at EOF (the previously-committed file had been manually trimmed at some point). The `ts_bindings_up_to_date` test normalises via `str::trim_end` so this doesn't cause a CI failure, and `bindings.ts` is excluded from biome's check via `"!src/lib/bindings.ts"` in `biome.json`. Benign — accept the wider whitespace diff.

## Session 522 — Backend LOW-severity cleanup batch — close 7 items (L-18, L-26, L-40, L-54, L-57, L-58, L-96) (2026-04-28)

**7 backend LOW-severity REVIEW-LATER items closed in one PROMPT.md batch.** Four parallel build subagents on file-disjoint subsystems (Cache+Pagination / Sync backoff docs / Bug-report hardening / URL credential strip); four pipelined reviewers, all PASS. Three reviewer micro-fixes applied: 4 edge-case tests added to L-96 (port+userinfo, multiple `@`, empty userinfo); a 2-line stale comment fixed in `sync_scheduler.rs::backoff_doubles_on_consecutive_failures` adjacent to the L-58 work; a clippy `cast_possible_truncation` fix in the new L-54 test. The L-18 builder DEVIATED from the spec's struct-field approach for the Cursor versioning (would have cascaded to 11+ struct-literal callsites breaking the strict file-scope) — implemented version logic at the encode/decode boundary via `serde_json::Value` intermediate, functionally equivalent for the future-bump-rejects-old-cursors guarantee. Reviewer accepted the deviation. The L-54 builder discovered and fixed a latent log-file sort bug — dated rolled files were sorted oldest-first via plain alphabetic, contradicting the "today first, then reverse-chrono" comment.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**

- **LOW findings:** 83 → 76 (7 items resolved).
- **Top-level open count (FEAT/MAINT/PERF/PUB/UX tracker):** 41 → 41.
- **Previously-resolved counter:** 626+ → 633+ across 489 sessions (latest session 522).

**Items closed (7):**

| Item | Subsystem / files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| L-18 | Cache+Pagination (`pagination/mod.rs` + `pagination/tests.rs`) | Cursor versioning at the encode/decode boundary. New `const CURRENT_CURSOR_VERSION: u8 = 1;`. `Cursor::encode` round-trips through `serde_json::to_value(self)?`, inserts `"version":CURRENT_CURSOR_VERSION` into the resulting `Object`, then base64-encodes the JSON. `Cursor::decode` parses back to `Value`, reads the `version` slot (defaulting to `CURRENT_CURSOR_VERSION` when absent so pre-versioning cursors decode cleanly), validates against current, then `serde_json::from_value(value)` deserialises into the unchanged `Cursor` struct. **Deviation from spec's struct-field approach:** adding `version: u8` to `Cursor` would have cascaded to 11+ `Cursor { ... }` struct-literal callsites across `tag_query`, `fts`, `commands`, `backlink` — breaking the strict file-scope. Wire format and future-bump guarantee are identical to the struct-field approach. 3 new tests pin the contract: unknown-version rejection, encode-sets-1, old-format-defaults-to-1. |
| L-26 | Cache+Pagination (`cache/projected_agenda.rs`) | **Doc-only fix.** Added a thorough doc-comment block above `rebuild_projected_agenda_cache` covering: (1) `today` and the 365-day horizon are anchored to the device's local timezone via `chrono::Local::now`; (2) multi-device users in different timezones see slight per-device divergence around midnight, with a concrete TZ-A vs TZ-B example; (3) divergence is acceptable under the single-user threat model — agenda projections are an eventually-consistent local cache that self-corrects on the next rebuild past the time skew; (4) why the doc-path was chosen over UTC normalization (per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability"); (5) cross-reference to `REVIEW-LATER.md` L-26. The `chrono::Local::now()` calls themselves are unchanged. New `projected_agenda_local_timezone_doc_present` test uses `include_str!` to assert the doc-block is in the source, providing a regression guard against silent doc-block deletion. |
| L-40 | Bug-report (`commands/bug_report.rs`) | New `is_error_or_warn_line(line: &str) -> bool` helper checks `" ERROR "` / `" WARN "` only in the FIRST 40 BYTES of the line (where the level lives in the standard tracing-subscriber format `<ISO-Z timestamp><space><level (right-padded to 5)><space><target>: <msg>` — level is at byte 27-35). `extract_recent_errors` delegates to the helper instead of inlining the full-line substring check. **Closes the false-positive class** where an INFO-level log whose body contained `" ERROR "` was being misclassified. Three new tests: positive matches for ERROR + WARN, false-positive rejection for INFO body containing ERROR after byte 40, empty-input safety. Builder chose prefix-bound over regex because the existing in-file test fixtures use a synthesized non-Z timestamp format that a strict ISO-Z regex would have rejected (forcing test churn). |
| L-54 | Bug-report (`commands/bug_report.rs`) | New `const MAX_BUNDLE_BYTES: usize = 10 * 1024 * 1024;` (10 MiB, matching GitHub's default issue-attachment limit). New `apply_bundle_cap` helper iterates entries newest-first, maintains `total_bytes.saturating_add(len)` cumulative counter, drops entries that would push past the cap, and synthesizes a `[skipped {N} older logs — bundle exceeded {cap_mb} MB cap]` marker entry when `skipped_count > 0`. Marker entry's `contents` is empty so it doesn't itself contribute to the byte total. **Latent ordering bug fixed in the same diff**: dated rolled files were sorted oldest-first via plain alphabetic on `agaric.log.YYYY-MM-DD` filenames, contradicting the "today first, then reverse-chrono" comment. New comparator special-cases undated `agaric.log` first then reverse-alphabetic on dated suffixes (`bn.cmp(an)` = newest-first). The only existing order-asserting test had just 2 entries (today + yesterday) where both sorts produced identical output — no regression. Two new tests: under-cap includes-all, over-cap drops + marker. |
| L-57 | Sync (`sync_scheduler.rs`) | **Doc-only fix.** Replaced `record_failure`'s doc-comment with an expanded block distinguishing the internal `state.backoff` (deterministic doubling: `1s → 2s → 4s → 8s → 16s → 32s → 60s`) from the wall-clock `next_retry_at = now + state.backoff * jitter` (where `jitter ∈ [0.9, 1.1]`). Includes a worked example: `state.backoff = 8s` → actual retry in `[now + 7.2s, now + 8.8s]`. Notes the 1s seed is overwritten in the very same call so callers never observe `1s` after `record_failure` returns. No behavioural change. |
| L-58 | Sync (`sync_scheduler.rs`) | **Doc-only fix** (option (b) chosen — option (a), changing the seed value, would have been a behavioural shift). Updated the `MIN_BACKOFF` constant's doc-comment to name the actual user-observed sequence `2,4,8,16,32,60s` in a code block, explicitly stating that `MIN_BACKOFF = 1s` is an internal seed never written to `next_retry_at`. Added an inline comment in `record_failure` near the doubling line clarifying "first call doubles 1s → 2s; sequence external observers see is 2,4,8,...". Bonus: 3-line doc-comment added to `MAX_BACKOFF` for symmetry; reviewer also fixed a 2-line stale comment in `backoff_doubles_on_consecutive_failures` that read `// 1s -> 2s -> 4s (backoff after 3 failures)` — incomplete and contradicting the next line's assertion of `8s`. New test `min_backoff_seed_doubles_to_two_seconds_on_first_failure` pins the contract. No behavioural change. |
| L-96 | Search & Links (`link_metadata/html_parser.rs` + `link_metadata/tests.rs`) | New `strip_userinfo(authority: &str) -> &str` helper splits on `/`, `?`, `#` to find the authority end, then `rfind('@')` on the authority slice (so an `@` in path/query/fragment is correctly ignored). Both `extract_origin` and `extract_domain` now call the helper after the `://` separation but BEFORE the port-strip, which transparently fixes a latent secondary bug: in `extract_domain`'s `host.rfind(':')` port-strip block, an input like `https://user:pwd@host.com:443/` would previously land `rfind(':')` on the userinfo `:` instead of the port `:`. Strip-first now feeds a clean `host:port` to the rfind. **Defense-in-depth**: cached `link_metadata.favicon_url` rows and tracing output never carry userinfo. Six builder tests + four reviewer-added edge-case tests (port + userinfo, multiple `@` in userinfo, empty userinfo, port preservation) cover the full surface. No new dependencies (string ops only — no `url` crate). |

**Files touched (this session's batch — 7 modified):**

- Backend Cache+Pagination (3): `src-tauri/src/pagination/{mod,tests}.rs`, `src-tauri/src/cache/projected_agenda.rs`
- Backend Bug-report (1): `src-tauri/src/commands/bug_report.rs`
- Backend Sync (1): `src-tauri/src/sync_scheduler.rs`
- Backend Search & Links (2): `src-tauri/src/link_metadata/{html_parser,tests}.rs`
- Docs (2): `REVIEW-LATER.md` (7 items removed; LOW count 83 → 76; previously-resolved counter bumped), `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry)

- L-18 / L-26: 71/71 cursor + projected_agenda + version tests pass; clippy clean.
- L-40 / L-54: 16/16 bug_report tests pass; clippy clean after the `try_from` micro-fix.
- L-57 / L-58: 24/24 backoff + record_failure tests pass.
- L-96: 12/12 userinfo + extract_origin + extract_domain tests pass; full 71-test `link_metadata::*` slice green.

Full-suite post-merge: `cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast` → **3083/3083 passed, 2 skipped**.

**Process notes:**

- **L-18 deviation explicitly preserved.** The reviewer recommended accepting the encode-via-Value pattern as functionally equivalent to a struct field for the contract that matters (future bumps reject old cursors). The deviation also kept the file-scope clean — the alternative would have required a cascading 11+ -file edit that no longer fit in a "S" cost item. If the codebase later wants the more-explicit struct-field approach, it can be a future reshape; the wire format is identical.
- **L-54 latent ordering bug found-and-fixed in the same batch.** The previous comparator was `a.0.file_name().cmp(&b.0.file_name())` (plain alphabetic), which sorted dated rolled files OLDEST-first despite the comment claiming "today first, then reverse-chrono". The L-54 cap walk needs newest-first to make "drop oldest first" trivially correct, so the L-54 builder fixed it in the same diff with a special-case for undated `agaric.log`. The only existing order-asserting test had just 2 entries (today + yesterday) where both sorts produce identical output, so no test regression — the fix was invisible to CI but visible to readers.
- **L-57 / L-58 fully doc-only by design.** Option (a) of L-58 (changing `MIN_BACKOFF` to `MIN_BACKOFF / 2`) would have been a behavioural shift (telemetry counters would shift from 2,4,8,... to 1,2,4,...). Per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability", we picked option (b) — describe the actual sequence in the docs. The companion test pins the user-observed `2s` first-failure wait so future regressions surface immediately.
- **L-96 reviewer added 4 edge-case tests** that the builder didn't include: port preservation in `extract_origin` and `extract_domain`, multiple `@` in userinfo (rightmost wins), and empty userinfo. The implementation already handled these cases correctly; only test coverage was missing.
- **No subagents canceled, no stash chaos.** Four worktrees, four parallel builds, four pipelined reviewers. Total wall-clock dominated by the slowest cold-compile (~5min). All four worktrees cleaned up after merge.

| L-39 | Commands/Op-log diagnostics (`commands/history.rs`) | `compute_edit_diff_inner` no longer propagates `serde_json::from_str` errors via bare `?` (which sanitised through `sanitize_internal_error` to a generic "internal error"). Now maps to `AppError::InvalidOperation(format!("op ({device_id}, {seq}) payload not parseable as EditBlockPayload: {e}"))`. `InvalidOperation` is in the pass-through set of `sanitize_internal_error` (verified at `commands/mod.rs:771-784`), so the diagnostic survives to the frontend / activity feed. Caller-driven (passive scheduler convention preserved — no background task spawned). Three tests pin idle removal, held-entry retention, and empty-map behaviour. The `try_lock_peer` race is mutex-protected: cloning happens inside the same `peer_locks.lock()` guard the GC must acquire, so strong_count cannot transiently dip to 1 mid-clone. Test-only `peer_locks_len()` accessor exposed under `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)`. |

New regression test pins the gating: `repeat_seq_not_incremented_without_repeat_count` creates a sibling without `repeat-count` and asserts both `!has_seq` AND `!has_count`. |

A type-loose write of `"2025-12-31T23:59:59Z"` would compare wrong (`T` > `-`) and the recurrence would never stop. Fix: invoke `crate::commands::is_valid_iso_date(until_str)` (already imported, strict 10-char shape with month/day range checks at `commands/properties.rs:430-448`) BEFORE the lex compare. On invalid input: `tracing::warn!` with structured `block_id` + `until_str` fields, return `Ok(false)` (stop recurrence — defense-in-depth, not silent loop). Four new tests cover past-due, future-due, timestamp-suffix (the regression case), and empty string. **Vehicle swap**: H-4's `set_todo_state_atomic_when_recurrence_fails` test in `commands/tests/property_cmd_tests.rs` and M-77's `handle_recurrence_propagates_set_property_error` test both used `repeat-until = "not-a-date"` as their corrupt-trigger vehicle; after L-100 that value short-circuits cleanly to `Ok(false)` instead of erroring. Replaced with whitespace `repeat = " "` planted directly into `block_properties`, which trips L-6's empty-`value_text` guard inside `set_property_in_tx` during the copy step — same propagation-via-`?` path. H-4 atomicity (no state transition / no `completed_at` / no sibling / op_log unchanged) and M-77 invariants both preserved. |

Now: `match e.classify()` produces a short structured category — `"data shape mismatch"` for `Category::Data` (the common case for missing-field / type-mismatch since `from_value` operates on a pre-parsed tree), `"json syntax"`, `"json truncated"`, `"json io"` — and the verbose serde error goes to `tracing::debug!`. Closed-set match with all 4 `Category` variants explicit (no `_ =>` catch-all). Three new tests + the L-123 anti-leak invariant (`!msg.contains("line")` / `!msg.contains("column")`) explicitly asserted. **Process notes:**

- **L-100 vehicle-swap deviation accepted.** Two existing tests outside `recurrence/compute.rs` (one for H-4, one for M-77) used the corrupt `repeat-until = "not-a-date"` as their failure-injection vehicle. The L-100 fix's purpose is to handle exactly that input gracefully, so both tests stopped failing where they were supposed to. Replacing the vehicle with whitespace `repeat = " "` (which trips L-6's empty-`value_text` guard via the same recurrence-internal propagation path) preserves the test invariants while letting both H-4 and M-77 continue to characterise their own concerns. The orchestrator authorized this single-file scope expansion in `property_cmd_tests.rs` because the alternative (deleting the H-4 atomicity test or accepting a permanently broken verification suite) was strictly worse.
- **L-86 caller swallow pattern preserved across two distinct call sites.** `sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs:594` already had `if let Err(e) = … { tracing::warn!(...) }` so the new `NotFound` flowed transparently through that. `commands/sync_cmds.rs::set_peer_address_inner` got an explicit `match &e { AppError::NotFound(_) => warn+swallow, _ => return Err(e) }` because it's a user-facing IPC where the regular "peer doesn't exist" case is already handled by an upstream pre-check; the new `NotFound` only fires on the TOCTOU race with `delete_peer_ref`.
- **L-123 dual-source closed-set match.** `serde_json::error::Category` has exactly 4 variants (`Io`, `Syntax`, `Data`, `Eof`) per the upstream `error.rs` source; the new match enumerates all four with no `_ =>` catch-all. Future variants would be a compile error, not a silent fallthrough. The `Syntax` / `Eof` / `Io` paths are technically unreachable from `parse_args` (the function takes a pre-parsed `serde_json::Value`, so JSON-text parse errors cannot arise here) but covering them in the match is still correct — pins the strings against future regressions where the input format might change.
- **L-99 doc-only outcome.** The "always bump `repeat-seq` when any repeat property is set" alternative would be a behavioural change requiring user approval per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability". Deferred. The doc block makes the current intent explicit so a future contributor understands the shape before reaching for that change.
- **Worktree-per-subagent paid off again.** Four `git worktree add --detach` checkouts kept builds independent; cold-compile cost was ~5 min per worktree but ran in parallel; total wall-clock ≈ slowest subagent. No stash chaos. All four worktrees cleaned up via `git worktree remove --force` after merge.
- **One subagent canceled in session 520, none in this session.** All four parallel build subagents completed cleanly. Pipelined reviewers also ran cleanly with two minor in-flight micro-fixes (L-86 tighter NotFound assertion, L-99/L-100 `cargo fmt`).

| Item | Subsystem / files | Change |

| L-16 | Materializer (`materializer/consumer.rs`) | `retry_with_backoff` no longer emits an `error`-level log on first-attempt failure. `log_consumer_result` gained a `level: tracing::Level` parameter; first-attempt and intermediate retry-attempt non-panic failures now log at `debug`. Panics still log at `error`. After the loop, a single `tracing::error!(label, error = msg, "error processing materializer task")` fires only when `!succeeded && !panicked`. Successful retries emit `tracing::info!(label, retry, "materializer task succeeded after retry")`. **`fg_errors` / `bg_errors` semantics unchanged** — still bumped by callers. Bg-side `run_background` is symmetric (uses the same helper). Two new tests use `LogBufWriter` (mirrors existing `WarnBufWriter` pattern, AGENTS.md "test helper duplication is intentional") to assert no `ERROR` substring on retry-success path and that `ERROR` appears on retry-exhaustion path. |
| L-46 | Commands/MCP (`commands/mcp.rs` + `commands/mod.rs` + `lib.rs`) | New `pub struct McpToggleGate(pub Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>>)` and parallel `McpRwToggleGate`, registered as Tauri managed state alongside `McpLifecycle` / `McpRwLifecycle`. Both `mcp_set_enabled` and `mcp_rw_set_enabled` Tauri commands acquire their respective gate via `let _guard = gate.0.lock().await` for the full command body so rapid UI toggles cannot interleave the marker write + `is_running()` check + `spawn_mcp_*_task` sequence. RO and RW use separate gates so they don't block each other. Three new tests pin: `set_marker_enabled_works_for_both_ro_and_rw` (helper parity), `mcp_toggle_gate_serializes_concurrent_callers` (3 concurrent callers, asserts `max_concurrent == 1`), `mcp_rw_toggle_gate_is_independent_of_ro_gate` (RO held + RW lock acquires immediately within 100 ms timeout). `mcp_set_enabled` reached 8 args after the new state param — `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` added per the existing 19-site codebase convention. |
| L-47 | Commands/MCP (`commands/mcp.rs`) | Extracted `set_marker_enabled(app_data_dir, lifecycle, marker_filename, log_label, enabled)` private helper; both `mcp_set_enabled_inner` and `mcp_rw_set_enabled_inner` collapsed to thin wrappers passing `MCP_RO_ENABLED_MARKER`/`"RO"` or `MCP_RW_ENABLED_MARKER`/`"RW"`. Log strings consolidated to `"MCP marker created/removed"` with the RO/RW distinction now a structured `label = "RO"|"RW"` field (easier to filter in tracing output). `lifecycle.shutdown()` still fires on disable in both paths. Status / disconnect-all / socket-path pairs left as-is (already trivial 1-line forwarders). |
| L-95 | Backlink (`backlink/query.rs` + `command_integration_tests/backlink_integration.rs`) | `eval_backlink_query` now adds `AND bl.source_id != ?1` to the base-set query, aligning with the existing convention in `eval_unlinked_references` and `eval_backlink_query_grouped`. `total_count` no longer inflates on self-links. The query is the runtime form (`query_scalar::<_, String>(...)`) — no `.sqlx/` regen needed. **Discovered**: the integration test `backlinks_filtered_self_referencing` was pinning the BUG's behavior (asserted `items.len() == 1` for a self-link); renamed to `backlinks_filtered_self_reference_excluded` and flipped to `items.len() == 0` + `total_count == 0` + `filtered_count == 0`, matching the three sibling grouped-path tests in `backlink/tests.rs`. Two new unit tests in `backlink/query.rs` cover the direct-INSERT path; the integration test exercises the materializer-driven path. |
| L-129 | GCal (`gcal_push/oauth.rs`) | `classify_refresh_error` rewritten as exhaustive closed-set categorization across all 7 `BasicErrorResponseType` variants and all 4 `RequestTokenError` variants. Zero `format!("...{err}")` interpolation — variant payloads (`Extension(_)`, `Other(_)`, `Request(_)`, `Parse(_, _)`) are matched with `_` and discarded. `B::InvalidGrant`/`B::UnauthorizedClient` still maps to `AppError::Gcal(GcalErrorKind::Unauthorized)`; everything else maps to `AppError::Validation("oauth.refresh_failed: <category>")` with the variant name only. Five new tests: two unauthorized happy-path pins + three anti-leak tests (sentinel string in `Other`, `Extension`, and named-variant payloads — assert sentinel is NOT in the resulting `AppError` message). |
| L-130 | GCal (`gcal_push/keyring_store.rs`) | `KeyringTokenStore::load` no longer propagates `serde_json::Error` via `?` (which would surface a position + context window of the input bytes — potentially partial token chars). Replaced with `.map_err(\|_\| AppError::Validation("keyring.malformed_blob".into()))?`. Existing test `keyring_store_load_returns_error_for_malformed_json` updated (it previously pinned the leaky `AppError::Json` shape). New test `keyring_load_with_malformed_json_returns_validation_without_token_chars` asserts the message is **exactly** `"keyring.malformed_blob"` and does NOT contain a `TOKEN_SENTINEL_ya29...` Google-access-token-shaped sentinel. |

**Process notes:**

- **Worktree-per-subagent paid off.** Four `git worktree add --detach` checkouts under `/tmp/agaric-wt-{core,mat,search,gcal}` gave each parallel build subagent its own `target/` directory; no cross-batch stash chaos like session 518's "five subagents stashing each other's work" scenario. Cold-compile cost was ~5 min per worktree but ran in parallel; total wall-clock build time ≈ slowest subagent (5m32s) rather than 4× sequential.
- **Reviewer-applied micro-fixes preserved by file-copy merge.** Both reviewer additions (L-91 inline comment, L-131 two extra tests) landed in their respective worktrees during review. The orchestrator merged via `cp $WORKTREE/<file> $MAIN/<file>` after verifying clean git status in each worktree — simpler and more auditable than `git apply` / cherry-pick across worktrees with a clean main tree, especially when changes are file-disjoint.
- **`debug_assert!` removed in L-14.** The original recommendation in REVIEW-LATER suggested either `Err` + `error!` log OR `debug_assert!(false, ...)`. The builder went with the simpler `Err`+`error!` path because adding `debug_assert!(false, ...)` to a code path that tests *intentionally* exercise (to assert the misroute Err contract) would have forced every such test to use `#[should_panic]`. The rationale comment is in place at `handlers.rs:93-96` / `:925-926`. The misroute is still observable through the `error!` log and the consumer's `fg_errors` / `bg_errors` counters.
- **Pre-existing `cargo clippy --all-targets` failure noted but out-of-scope.** Two reviewers flagged `src-tauri/src/sync_files.rs:1561` (`(i % 256) as u8` — `clippy::cast_possible_truncation`) when running clippy with `--all-targets`. The prek hook at `prek.toml:327` runs `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` *without* `--all-targets`, so test-code lints don't block the commit and this pre-existing warning has shipped through several sessions. Filing as informational; not part of this batch.
- **Stash management — no new stashes.** The 4 stale stashes from session 518 are still present; not touched in this commit (per session 518's recommendation, they should be reviewed and dropped together rather than piecemeal).
- **Worktree cleanup.** All four `/tmp/agaric-wt-*` worktrees removed via `git worktree remove` after the merge; the main repo returned to a single-worktree state.

One `cargo fmt` round-trip needed before final pass (5 long-line struct fields in `sync_protocol/types.rs::SyncMessage` enum got reflowed to single-line form, plus `peer_refs.rs` and `pairing.rs` doc-comment whitespace). The merged build was buildable (`cargo check --lib` passing) for most of the session despite parallel-batch chaos.

**Process notes:**

- **Working-tree chaos was the dominant story.** Five concurrent build subagents repeatedly stashed each other's in-flight files for build isolation, then `git stash pop`'d their own stashes — but those operations happened in interleaved windows where the stash content captured *other* batches' work too. Three subagents reported their edits were silently reverted at some point and had to be re-applied. `mcp/dispatch.rs` (a NEW file from MAINT-150) was inadvertently deleted by MAINT-151's stash-recovery dance and re-created by MAINT-150's later reapply. MAINT-151's sub-item (g) (`_emitter` removal in `classify_date_err` + `push_date`) was *entirely lost* during a stash recovery; the reviewer caught it and applied the inline fix. The orchestrator verified the final on-disk state via direct file reads + `cargo check --lib` before committing — all 5 batches' intent was preserved end-to-end despite the noise.
- **TODO for next session: parallel reviewers must NOT stash shared in-flight changes.** PROMPT.md should be updated to forbid `git stash` for build isolation in subagents. Better alternatives:
  1. Use `cargo nextest -E 'test(/regex/)'` filter syntax (the "package(agaric) and test(/foo/)" form) to scope tests within a multi-batch working tree.
  2. Use `cargo check --lib --quiet -p agaric` against a single binary if test compilation is broken from parallel work.
  3. Trust the orchestrator's full-prek run after merge — subagents don't need a green full build to verify their own subsystem changes.
- **Reviewer work was high-value.** Two of the five reviewers caught real defects: (i) MAINT-148's `cache/tags.rs` was missed by the builder for the `rebuild_with_timing` migration; reviewer applied the one-line fix. (ii) MAINT-151's `_emitter` removal was lost during stash chaos; reviewer re-applied the full removal across 5 sites in `connector.rs`. Without per-batch reviewers, both regressions would have shipped as silent inconsistencies.
- **MAINT-148(d) lifetime-constraint outcome.** The builder's first attempt at a fully generic sort helper (parameterized by both fetcher closure AND comparator) hit Rust lifetime issues with the borrowed `&[&str]` inside the future. The pragmatic resolution — keep per-column fetchers as discrete async fns and only generic-ify the comparator pass — is the correct trade-off: the comparator was the actual duplication anyway.
- **MAINT-149(a) latent bugfix.** While extracting `consume_binary_data`, the builder noticed it had no `size_bytes == 0` handling. Tracing the sender path showed `send_binary_chunked` *always* emits at least one frame (even for empty data, it sends a single empty `Binary` frame). The pre-fix `consume_binary_data`'s `while received < size_bytes` loop never iterates when `size_bytes == 0`, leaving the empty frame in the receive buffer to corrupt the next message. The fix drains exactly one empty frame when `size_bytes == 0`. Latent bug; whether anyone has hit it in practice depends on whether 0-byte attachments coincide with the unknown-attachment-id consume path.
- **MAINT-150(d) defer reasoning.** The schemars approach (compile-time link from `ListPagesArgs` struct to its JSON schema) would introduce a new dependency requiring user approval per AGENTS.md "Coupled Dependency Updates". Deferred to a future explicit decision; partial fix (2 equivalence tests pinning struct↔schema parity) is the bridging step.
- **MAINT-151(b) cleaner option chosen.** Between "Transport(String)" variant vs "documented `NO_RESPONSE: u16 = 0` constant", the builder picked the variant — cleaner because logs and `CycleOutcome` no longer print misleading "HTTP 0" for transport failures.
- **MAINT-152(a) macro is non-portable.** The `batch_insert_snapshot_rows!` macro accesses `tx` from the surrounding `apply_snapshot` body via Mixed Site hygiene. This works correctly for the function-local helper but means the macro is not reusable elsewhere. Acceptable given exactly one caller; flagged as a style note by reviewer.
- **Stale stash management** — at session-end the working tree had 4 stashes accumulated from inter-batch isolation:
  1. "More parallel batch work" (cache/tags.rs, snapshot/restore.rs, sync_daemon/{orchestrator,server}.rs)
  2. "Other batch parallel work" (error.rs, gcal_push/{api,connector}.rs, sync_files.rs)
  3. "WIP on main: 68a22b8 ..." (large in-flight dump from earlier in the session)
  4. "in-progress unrelated H-4/H-13" (pre-session 514 stash, kept across sessions)

  All 4 contain stale snapshots — every file they cover has since been re-edited and re-merged into the working tree. Recommend dropping all 4 in a follow-up cleanup; not done in this commit to avoid losing history if any of them happen to have unique content.

**5 backend MAINT items closed in one PROMPT.md-style batch.** Five parallel build subagents on file-disjoint subsystems (gcal_push, mcp, snapshot, tag_inheritance, commands), five pipelined reviewers, two minor inline fixes from the reviewers, biome auto-fix + 3 clippy fixes from the orchestrator, single commit. One subagent surfaced a real data-loss bug (MAINT-133 reproduced end-to-end before fixing); another authored a byte-exact equivalence test that pins the SQL semantics (MAINT-141).

- **MAINT-142** unblocked (its precondition MAINT-141 closed); description updated to note macros file is a sibling that stays in place.

Three orchestrator-side fixes were needed after the merge: `cargo fmt` reflowed long string literals in `tag_inheritance_macros.rs`; `cargo clippy` flagged 3 new lints from the merged code (auto-deref `&mut **tx` → `&mut tx` at 2 sites, `defaults.iter().any(|d| *d == value)` → `defaults.contains(&value)` at 1 site) — all fixed in 3 one-line edits.
