**Process notes:**

- **5-way disjoint backend split worked cleanly.** Each subagent owned a self-contained subsystem (gcal_push/connector.rs, mcp/, snapshot/, tag_inheritance.rs + new macros file, commands/) — no file conflicts despite five subagents writing to the same working tree concurrently. Diff-isolation per subagent was clean.
- **Reviewer race window for MAINT-141.** A parallel reviewer (MAINT-136's) stashed concurrent files including `tag_inheritance_macros.rs` to isolate `cargo nextest run mcp`; that reviewer's `git stash pop` succeeded, but the brief mid-stash window was when MAINT-141's reviewer ran. The reviewer reported the macros file as missing; investigation showed the file existed, was just transiently stashed. Re-launched MAINT-141's review with stash-aware preconditions (`ls -la` of the macros file + `git stash list` count check) at the start. The second reviewer verified everything correctly. **Process improvement:** parallel reviewers should not stash shared in-flight changes for build isolation; instead use `cargo nextest -E 'test(/foo/)'` filters or run `cargo build` only against a single binary (`-p agaric --bin agaric`).
- **MAINT-133 was a real bug.** The review note "before shipping a fix, confirm the bug is reachable" was the right framing — the Phase 1 failing test confirmed the data-loss path. Without that confirmation step, we'd have shipped a `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump for a phantom regression. Build subagent also caught the spec's claim about "the decoder tolerates missing fields" was conditional on `#[serde(default)]` — without it, ciborium hard-errors on missing keys, which would have shipped a worse regression than the original bug.
- **MAINT-141's byte-exact equivalence test is the right shape for SQL macro refactors.** Anyone editing a macro body and accidentally diverging from the pre-refactor SQL gets caught loudly by `macros_match_pre_maint141_sql_byte_for_byte`. The literal `100` is duplicated across 7 macro bodies (sqlx requires `&'static str` literals for `concat!`-composed SQL, so we can't embed the constant), but `macro_depth_literal_matches_constant` test guards drift between the constant and the macro literals.
- **`#[serde(default)]` is mandatory for additive struct fields read by ciborium.** A note for the next snapshot extension: ciborium's struct deserializer treats a missing CBOR map key as a hard error unless the field carries `#[serde(default)]`. The pre-existing `todo_state` / `priority` / `due_date` / `scheduled_date` fields all have it; the new `conflict_type` followed the same pattern.
- **The new `tauri-command-sanitize` hook is now part of prek.** Future `#[tauri::command]` wrappers returning `Result<_, AppError>` and using `?` propagation must end with `.map_err(sanitize_internal_error)` — the IPC boundary never leaks bare `AppError::Sqlx` / `AppError::Internal` to the renderer. The 7-entry allowlist documents pre-existing exemptions (mostly `mcp.rs` commands and `logging.rs:get_log_dir`) and explicitly forbids new additions.
- **MAINT-147 sub-items (d) and (f) were stale.** The cited `dispatch_background_for_*` helpers no longer exist; MAINT-112 phase B replaced them with `CommandTx::commit_and_dispatch`. The build subagent correctly noted "the duplication this sub-item targeted has already been fixed" and skipped both. Items in REVIEW-LATER can drift; the SKIP-with-rationale pattern is the right way to handle that.
- **Specta bindings regenerate on doc-comment changes.** MAINT-147(k) added 12 new `///` doc comments on `#[tauri::command]` wrappers; tauri-specta's TypeScript generator picks these up, so `src/lib/bindings.ts` regenerated. This caused an initial `specta_tests::ts_bindings_up_to_date` failure that was resolved by running `cargo test --release --lib specta_tests -- --ignored` to write the new bindings.

Plus the orchestrator-side workflow fix from earlier in the session (single commit, separate from this batch):

- `.github/workflows/release.yml` — `save-if: false` added to both `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` invocations (desktop matrix + Android job) so cache-save flakes can never block a release. Released as commit `4229115` and recovered the 0.1.16 APK by retagging at the new commit (run 25009988305).

**Verification:** `prek run --all-files` → all 33 hooks PASS (vitest 8546 tests across 318 test files, cargo nextest 3000+ tests, full clippy / fmt / biome / tsc / lychee / sqruff / cargo deny / cargo machete / sqlx prepare check). Biome `--write` was needed once after the merge to apply formatter + import-sort auto-fixes to 7 of the 35 files (long array literals reflowed, import order normalized in `useBlockCollapse.test.ts` and `useBlockDnD.test.ts`); after that, `npx biome check` exits 0 with only 9 pre-existing `suppressions/unused` + cognitive-complexity warnings on files this batch did NOT touch (`SearchablePopover.tsx`, `AttachmentRenderer.tsx`, `JournalPage.tsx`, `JournalPage.test.tsx`, `JournalCalendarDropdown.test.tsx`, `test-setup.ts`).

**Process notes:**

- **5-way subagent split with no file overlap.** TEST-56 + TEST-61 + TEST-63 had heavy file-set overlap (the same hook test files were targets of multiple items). Rather than worktree, the orchestrator partitioned the 35-file work surface into 5 disjoint subsets — A (6 hook files: TEST-56 + TEST-61 + TEST-63 partial), B (9 hook files: TEST-61 + TEST-63 partial), C (4 lib files: TEST-61 + TEST-64 partial), D (3 hook + 3 lib: TEST-62 + TEST-63 partial), E (10 component/editor/lib: TEST-64). All 5 ran in parallel in the main tree without conflicts.
- **Pipelined reviews.** As builders A, C, D, E finished (close together), their reviewers were launched immediately in parallel rather than waiting for all 5 builders. Reviewer B was the only sequential one (B was the longest builder, finishing last). Review wall-clock was bounded by the slowest reviewer (~2-3 min), not 5×reviewer-cost serial.
- **Zero inline reviewer fixes.** All 5 reviewers approved their respective batches without needing to apply fixes. The reviewer for E pre-emptively predicted the biome formatter would flag the `property-picker.test.ts:287` extra blank line — confirmed when prek ran post-merge and biome `--write` reflowed it.
- **9 pre-existing biome warnings surfaced but did not block.** The first prek run failed only on biome formatter errors caused by my long-array literal call sites; the 9 `suppressions/unused` + cognitive-complexity warnings existed on `main` before my changes (verified by stashing the working tree and re-running biome check on `4229115`). They are pre-existing tech debt from a biome bump that retired/renamed `lint/performance/noDelete` (and similar). Biome itself exits 0 on warnings, so the prek hook passes once the formatter errors are fixed.
- **Build subagents reported good observations the prompt didn't ask for.** Subagent C noticed the spec's claim that "the rest of the file already uses `vi.useFakeTimers()`" was wrong for `agenda-sort.test.ts` (true for `agenda-filters.test.ts` only) and added the fake-timer setup fresh, mirroring `agenda-filters.test.ts`'s pattern. Subagent C also flagged that the spec's `'2025-01-15T12:00:00Z'` UTC suffix would break in UTC+13/+14 timezones because `groupByDate` uses local `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate`; switched to `'2025-01-15T12:00:00'` (no `Z`). Both decisions were the right shape, not boilerplate compliance.
- **Reviewer E's site-selection disposition was the main judgment call.** TEST-64's spec line numbers for `BlockInlineControls.test.tsx:519-525` were stale; the closest axe site in the same nested describe was line 535 (overflow button — async Tooltip positioning). Builder E applied the timeout-bump there and left two other axe sites (default-state at 591, with-children at 599) untouched per the spec's "only these 3 sites" constraint. Reviewer E verified those untouched sites are synchronous renders without portals/tooltips, so they are not plausibly cold-load — the picked site was correct.
- **Windows release fix unrelated to this batch but resolved in same session.** The 0.1.16 release (commit `81c4b1f`) lost its Android APK because the Windows job's post-step cache save flaked and the dependency `android-build-and-release` (which `needs:` the desktop matrix) was skipped. Fixed in commit `4229115` with `save-if: false` on both `rust-cache@v2` steps. Old draft + tag deleted (`gh release delete 0.1.16 --cleanup-tag`), retagged at the fix commit, new release run is in flight at run 25009988305 — Windows + Android jobs are now decoupled from cache flakes.

- **MAINT-162** (ARIA role re-evaluation: `role="listbox"` → `role="grid"`) — M-cost architectural change requiring manual NVDA/VoiceOver verification per component (~6 components × manual AT testing). Not feasible in an automated session. The item's own description says "may be deferred behind MAINT-128 or shipped earlier as a per-file role flip" — preferring per-component work as those files are touched.

**Process notes:**

- **Discovery agents' calibration was systematically off.** The previous session's audit (which produced MAINT-153..162) had four discovery subagents collectively flag "1 SUSPECT out of ~600 exceptions" — adversarial validators inverted the bias and surfaced the real findings. Two-stage discovery + adversarial validation was the right shape for an exception audit; pure single-pass discovery would have shipped a "100% LEGITIMATE" verdict that was demonstrably false.
- **Build agent → reviewer pipelining worked.** Per PROMPT.md, reviewers were launched as builders finished rather than waiting for all builds. The MAINT-160 build → review → fix cycle caught 4 substantive issues (wrong `privacy_mode` default, `disconnect_gcal` argument-handling, comment count drift, missing `is_conflict` filter on the trash mock) and 1 missing-test gap, all addressed in a single fix subagent before merge. Without the review, `privacy_mode: 'titles_only'` would have shipped to e2e tests as a wrong default vs. Rust.
- **`#[allow]` for `f64 → i64` casts is a real std limitation.** The build subagent correctly identified that std lacks `TryFrom<f64>` for integer types and that the prek clippy gate is `-D warnings`, so removing the `#[allow]` would fail the gate. The 5 kept sites had their comments rewritten to be more explicit ("no `TryFrom` in std; the cast is safe because …") — better than the original "documents an invariant the cast doesn't enforce" pattern.
- **The e2e regex change is a no-op in current dev.** `index.html` includes `<link rel="icon" type="image/svg+xml" href="/favicon.svg">` and `public/favicon.svg` exists, so the favicon 404 doesn't fire. The new tight regex remains correct insurance for the day someone removes the favicon — but it doesn't change observed test behaviour today. This is the right shape: a regex that matches the literal Chromium error string for the genuine non-error noise, not a broad sieve that catches real bugs.
- **One intentional `_resetRateLimits` test→logger-internals coupling.** The `ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` count-assertion approach required clearing the logger's rate-limit map between tests because the boundary always logs the same `module:message` key. The internal `_resetRateLimits` helper has the right semantics (already used by `logger.test.ts`) and is `@internal`-tagged "Exported for testing only". Alternatives (per-test unique error messages, or fewer per-test count assertions) were considered and rejected as worse trade-offs. Documented in `ErrorBoundary.test.tsx`'s `beforeEach` rationale comment.
- **Rust flaky test (`run_file_transfer_initiator_breaks_on_cancel_m47`)** failed first-try in the post-MAINT-155/156 nextest run but passed on retry. Verified pre-existing: 5/5 PASS in isolation. Cast-syntax conversions in `sync_files.rs` test fixtures cannot affect the test's runtime behaviour (`usize → i64/u64` is a syntactic change for non-negative usize, not a value change). The flake is a contention race under full-suite parallelism — orthogonal to this batch.

This session investigated the WIP, finished the missing pieces for H-4, accepted H-13 as already complete, dropped H-9b (incomplete + scope too large for this session — left open in REVIEW-LATER), and added a dedicated H-4 atomicity regression test.

- **H-9b stays open** — left in the BCR appendix at H-9 (parent) / H-9b / H-9c entries; H-9b is still M-cost and needs careful safe-token-set tuning.

**Items closed (2):**

| Item | Files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| H-4 | `src-tauri/src/commands/blocks/crud.rs` (+85L: new `delete_property_in_tx`), `src-tauri/src/commands/blocks/mod.rs` + `commands/mod.rs` (re-exports), `src-tauri/src/commands/properties.rs` (the previous agent's WIP — `set_todo_state_inner` now opens one `CommandTx::begin_immediate` covering state change + timestamp writes + recurrence sibling creation, snapshots GCal pre-state inside that tx, and routes through `set_property_in_tx` / `delete_property_in_tx` / `handle_recurrence_in_tx`), `src-tauri/src/recurrence/compute.rs` (split `handle_recurrence` into a thin pool-based wrapper + new `handle_recurrence_in_tx(tx, device_id, block_id)` that takes a caller-managed `CommandTx`; bumped `&mut **tx` → `&mut ***tx` and `&mut tx,` → `&mut *tx,` since `tx` is now a `&mut CommandTx` parameter rather than an owned value), `src-tauri/src/recurrence/mod.rs` (re-export `handle_recurrence_in_tx`, drop crate-wide re-export of `handle_recurrence` since no production caller remains), `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/property_cmd_tests.rs` (new test `set_todo_state_atomic_when_recurrence_fails` plants a corrupt `repeat-until` value, asserts the rolled-back tx leaves todo_state unchanged + no completed_at + no recurrence sibling + op_log size unchanged). The `delete_property_in_tx` helper mirrors `delete_property_core` (validate block exists → append DeleteProperty op → clear column for reserved keys / DELETE FROM block_properties for non-reserved) but runs entirely inside the caller's tx and returns the OpRecord for the caller to enqueue. | One commit |
| H-13 | `src-tauri/migrations/0036_op_log_immutability_triggers.sql` (NEW — adds `_op_log_mutation_allowed` sentinel table + BEFORE UPDATE / BEFORE DELETE triggers on `op_log` that ABORT unless the sentinel is present), `src-tauri/src/op_log.rs` (NEW `enable_op_log_mutation_bypass` / `disable_op_log_mutation_bypass` helper pair; existing test `op_log_update_not_blocked_by_schema` rewritten to assert the trigger now fires; 2 new tests `compaction_path_with_bypass_succeeds` + `compaction_bypass_does_not_leak_to_sibling_connection` covering the documented enable → mutate → disable → commit dance and WAL connection-isolation guarantee), `src-tauri/src/snapshot/create.rs` (production compaction path wraps its per-device DELETEs with the bypass helper pair), `src-tauri/src/snapshot/restore.rs` (snapshot RESET path wraps the `DELETE FROM op_log` with the bypass helper pair), `src-tauri/src/dag/tests.rs` + `src-tauri/src/snapshot/tests.rs` + `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/tests.rs` (existing tests that DELETE from op_log directly migrated to use the bypass dance). The previous agent's `temp.` per-connection-scoped sentinel had been rejected by SQLite (triggers cannot reference temp-schema tables); the doc comment in the migration now documents that we achieve equivalent isolation via WAL semantics + transactional discipline (sibling connections never observe the sentinel as present because BEGIN IMMEDIATE serialises writers and the helper requires the sentinel be DELETEd before commit). | Same commit |

**H-9b dropped (left open):** the previous agent had added ~233L of deny-list redaction scaffolding to `src-tauri/src/commands/bug_report.rs` — a `SafeTokenClass` enum with 11 variants, ten compile-time regex matchers, an `allowed_classes_for_field` helper, a `RedactionContext` struct (separate MAINT-147(i) refactor) — but had NOT wired any of it into the existing `redact_line` function. Without the wiring, every component was dead code (15 unused-warning lines on `cargo build --lib`). Wiring it correctly requires careful tuning of the safe-token set + extensive property tests + a decision on `RedactionContext`'s adoption (which is a refactor separable from H-9b). All of that is M+-cost work and properly belongs in a dedicated session, not appended to a salvage commit. The bug_report.rs file was reverted to HEAD; H-9b stays open in the BCR appendix.

**Verification:** `cargo nextest run` → **2996 tests passed across 3 binaries (+1 new H-4 atomicity test vs session 513's 2995)**. `cargo sqlx prepare -- --tests` succeeded; `.sqlx/` cache regenerated (no stale entries). All 33 prek hooks pass (cargo fmt fixed two pre-existing minor format issues during the run; re-staged and reran clean).

**Process notes:**

- **The user spotted that the leftover Rust WIP was being skirted around session after session.** Sessions 511, 512, 513 had each stashed it to run prek and restored it after committing — never actually engaging with the work. Investigation revealed three substantial pieces (H-4, H-13, H-9b) at very different completeness levels: H-13 was production-ready, H-4 was 90% there but missing one helper function (`delete_property_in_tx`) and the recurrence-handler-as-`_in_tx`-variant split, H-9b was framework-only (declarations without wiring). Salvaging H-4 + H-13 in one session was tractable; salvaging H-9b was not.
- **Borrow checker subtlety on the recurrence split.** With `tx: &mut CommandTx` (parameter) vs the original `let mut tx = CommandTx::...` (owned value), every `&mut **tx` had to become `&mut ***tx` (one extra deref) and every `&mut tx,` argument had to become `&mut *tx,` (reborrow). The doc comment in `db.rs:166` mentioning that `&mut CommandTx` coerces to `&mut Transaction<'_>` "automatically" is true at the *function-call site* via deref-coercion-on-reborrow, but raw `**tx` / `***tx` for `Executor` access still needs the right deref count.
- **The session-count fix** ("165 sessions" → "482 sessions") landed in its own small commit before the H-4/H-13 work to keep the diff focused. The number had been getting +1 per session for 22+ sessions without ever matching the actual SESSION-LOG.md count — a documentation-drift cluster the user flagged at the same time.
- **`cargo sqlx prepare -- --tests` is the canonical command for this repo** (per AGENTS.md). The earlier attempt with bare `cargo sqlx prepare` (no `--tests`) deleted ~10 test-only `.sqlx/` cache entries; restored via `git checkout HEAD -- src-tauri/.sqlx/` and re-prepared with `--tests`.
- **Tests in `compute.rs:tests_h17_m77` still call the pool-based `handle_recurrence` directly.** The wrapper was preserved (with `#[allow(dead_code)]`) rather than rewriting the test bodies to construct a `CommandTx` themselves — keeps the existing M-77 / H-17 atomicity tests passing without any change. If a future cleanup wants to drop `handle_recurrence` entirely, those two tests need to be refactored to call `handle_recurrence_in_tx` inside a fresh `CommandTx` they manage.

| TEST-55 | `src/hooks/__tests__/useBatchCounts.test.ts:108-154` | Rewrote vacuous "cancels fetch on unmount" test using **option (c)** — two sibling hook instances sharing one promise. Builder verified React 19.2.5 + jsdom emits ZERO `console.error` / `console.warn` on unmounted-component setState (option (a) would have been vacuous), then implemented option (c): mock `countAgendaBatchBySource` returns one shared `Promise`, both `hookA` and `hookB` consume it, `hookA` is unmounted, `act(() => resolveAgenda(...))` resolves, `waitFor` asserts `hookB` received the data (`{ '2025-01-06': 99 }` / `{ 'page-1': 7 }`). Defensive `console.error` spy retained for a future React version that might reintroduce the warning. **Acknowledged limitation:** if the production cancellation guard at `useBatchCounts.ts:28-37` were removed, the test would still pass because `hookB`'s data-arrival assertions are independent of the unmounted hook's setState calls. The test's primary value is now exercising the promise-resolution path on both hooks; cancellation regressions remain hard to catch without more invasive instrumentation that would itself be fragile. Documented in the test file. |

Eliminates the cascade-failure vector where a thrown assertion in either offline-test would leave `navigator.onLine === false` for the remaining 20+ tests. Pattern mirrors `src/hooks/__tests__/useOnlineStatus.test.ts:1-10`. |
| TEST-58 | `src/lib/__tests__/keyboard-config.test.ts:1, 34-36, 268-303` | Added `afterEach` import + one top-level `afterEach(() => vi.restoreAllMocks())` alongside the existing `beforeEach(() => localStorage.clear())`. If any prior `expect()` in those tests had thrown, `Storage.prototype.setItem` / `removeItem` would have stayed stubbed-to-throw for the remaining ~1080 lines (and the next `beforeEach`'s `localStorage.clear()` would have masqueraded as setup failure). |
| TEST-59 | `src/editor/__tests__/use-roving-editor.test.ts:540-544` | Replaced `await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 50))` in the `setup()` helper with `await waitFor(() => expect(hook.result.current.editor).not.toBeNull())`. AGENTS.md "No timing hacks" rule. The 50 ms hack is not guaranteed under CI worker contention. All 6 downstream callers of `setup()` already dereference / assert non-null on `result.current.editor`, so `editor !== null` is the correct readiness signal. |
| TEST-60 | `src/lib/__tests__/export-graph.test.ts:2, 41-77` | Replaced single vacuous `expect(blob).toBeInstanceOf(Blob)` assertion with real ZIP inspection using `JSZip.loadAsync(await blob.arrayBuffer())`. Added `import JSZip from 'jszip'` (already a direct dep). Test fixture now uses two realistic 26-char Crockford-base32 ULIDs for the two duplicate-named pages. Three new assertions: `sameNameMd.toHaveLength(2)` (catches collapse/overwrite), `new Set(sameNameMd).size === 2` (catches duplicate filenames), `regex /_[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{8}\.md$/` (catches wrong slice length). **Production-format adaptation:** spec hint suggested 26-char ULID suffix, but `src/lib/export-graph.ts:23` actually does `${name}_${page.id.slice(0, 8)}` — assertions match the actual 8-char Crockford slice. Mutation analysis confirms: removing `seen.has(name)` guard, changing `slice(0, 8)` to `slice(0, 4)` or `slice(0, 26)`, all caught by the new assertions. |

**8 items closed in one batched session — second batch of the user-defined Tier 1 ("highest ROI, low risk").** Five parallel build subagents on largely non-overlapping files (UX-1+UX-2+UX-6 partial; UX-5+UX-8+UX-2-table; UX-6 contrast; UX-9; MAINT-115+UX-4+UX-6 overdue chip), paired with five review subagents — all SHIP. Three orchestrator-level fixes were necessary post-build:

1. **Reviewer A flagged a hardcoded aria-label** in `PageTreeItem.tsx:145` (`Create page under ${node.fullPath}`) that should have been `t('pageTree.createUnder', { path })`. Fixed in-place; the `pageTree.createUnder` i18n key already existed.
2. **Reviewer C flagged a WCAG AA contrast failure** in the SpaceManageDialog accent-swatch Check icon: `text-white` falls below 4.5:1 against `amber-500` (≈1.83:1) and `violet-500` (≈3.82:1). Resolved by adding `strokeWidth={3}` (matches the established Checkbox primitive idiom in the repo) plus `drop-shadow-[0_0_1.5px_rgb(0_0_0/0.9)]` halo, which guarantees readability against every Tailwind 500 swatch fill.
3. **Builder E flagged a nested-interactive a11y violation** in StaticBlock.test.tsx: Builder B's UX-5 fix added `role="button"` to the AttachmentRenderer image wrapper, but that wrapper now contained focusable `ImageResizeToolbar` buttons → axe `nested-interactive`. Resolved by dropping `role="button"` on the wrapper while keeping the `aria-label` and `tabIndex={0}` for keyboard access; restored the two `biome-ignore` comments (`noStaticElementInteractions`, `noNoninteractiveTabindex`) with explanatory rationale. Updated the corresponding UX-5 test to assert the absence of `role="button"` instead.
4. **Builder E flagged a double-toast UX issue**: DateChipEditor already fires `toast.success(t('dateChip.dateUpdated'))` + `announce` on save (existing behaviour), so AgendaResults' new UX-4 `toast.success(t('agenda.dateSaved'))` + `announce` would have produced two toasts on inline date edits inside the agenda. Resolved by dropping the redundant toast/announce pair from AgendaResults' `onSuccess` (DateChipEditor already covers UX-4's "user knows the save succeeded" intent). Removed the now-dead `agenda.dateSaved` and `announce.dateSaved` i18n keys plus the `toast` and `announce` imports from AgendaResults.

Catching them at orchestration is faster than spawning a sixth "integration" subagent.
- **MAINT-115 explicitly partial.** Only wave (a) — the UX-visible `catch { toast.error(...) }` sites — landed this session. Waves (b) hook-layer silent catches and (c) lib-layer fallbacks are deferred. The utility is in place; future migrations are mechanical.

Reviewer C's Pass-1 grep confirmed it. Worth filing separately if it surfaces as user-visible (today the line just renders without the styling, gracefully).

All 5 `set_*` mutators share `op_type='set_property'` and differ only in the payload's `key`. Pattern follows TEST-41 already shipped for tag commands. |

New `forks: usize` field on `ApplyResult`. **Bulk SELECT before txn** (subagent's deviation from per-op-in-txn — per-op SELECTs caused intermittent `SQLITE_BUSY` in `large_op_log_incremental_sync` because they extended the BEGIN IMMEDIATE write-lock proportional to batch size). Bulk SELECT uses `json_each(?)` over the batch's `(device_id, seq)` pairs and builds a HashMap for O(1) per-op lookup. On hash mismatch: `tracing::warn!` with peer/local hashes + bumps `result.forks`. `INSERT OR IGNORE` retained as safety net. 2 new regression tests (`apply_remote_ops_detects_fork_with_same_seq_different_hash`, `apply_remote_ops_does_not_count_real_duplicates_as_forks`). Reviewer confirmed TOCTOU-safe for Phase 1 pairwise sync — `apply_remote_ops` is the only writer for remote `(device_id, seq)` slots; `append_local_op_at` only writes the local device_id; per-peer mutex prevents concurrent syncs with same peer. Phase 4 (mesh) would need re-evaluation, documented in code. |
| UX-3 | 5 components + `src/lib/i18n.ts` | 4 inline `<p>` empty states migrated to `<EmptyState compact ... />`: PageTagSection, PageOutline, DiffDisplay, DaySection (compact mode). | UX-7 | `src/lib/repeat-utils.ts`, `src/lib/i18n.ts`, `src/components/PairingEntryForm.tsx`, plus 3 callsites | `formatRepeatLabel(value)` → `formatRepeatLabel(value, t: TFunction)`. 12 new `repeat.*` i18n keys (with `_one` / `_other` plural conventions matching existing pattern). `PairingEntryForm` ordinals (`['1st','2nd','3rd','4th']`) replaced with 4 `pairing.ordinal.*` keys. Byte-identical visible output; existing tests pass without modification. Orchestrator added a documented `as unknown as TFunction` cast in `useBlockSlashCommands.ts` to bridge the file-local loose `TFn = (...args: any[]) => any` type alias and the new strict `TFunction` parameter. |

Verified all 4 new safeguard tests are non-tautological — each would fail if the corresponding safeguard regressed.
- The **H-14 reviewer** traced both initiator and responder code paths to confirm `apply_remote_ops` is the sole writer for remote `(device_id, seq)` slots (no TOCTOU window in Phase 1). Cross-checked `append_local_op_at` to confirm it only writes the local device_id. Documented the Phase 4 mesh-sync caveat.
- The **TEST-42 reviewer** confirmed each op_type string against `op.rs:63-64` (`SetProperty => "set_property"`, `DeleteProperty => "delete_property"`) and verified the count=2 assertions for set+clear paths (both clear paths go through `set_property_inner`, not `delete_property_inner`).

- The **UX-7 reviewer** confirmed all 16 new i18n keys are referenced in code (no dead keys), verified pluralization works via `t(key, { count })` matching the existing `_one`/`_other` convention, and confirmed PairingEntryForm tests pass unchanged because the new key values produce byte-identical output.

**Process notes:**

- **First Tier 1 batch under user-defined priorities.** The user issued an explicit five-tier ordering of remaining work; this is batch 1 of Tier 1 ("highest ROI, low risk"). Tier 1 has 13 items total — TEST-45 already closed in session 510, the remaining 12 will need at least one more batch (the next batch is MAINT-115 + remaining UX-1/2/4/5/6/8/9 — 8 items).
- **Subagent rule conflicts escalate to orchestrator.** Same pattern as session 510. UX-7's signature change broke a pre-existing TS error in `useBlockSlashCommands.ts` (a `TFn = (...any) => any` loose alias that doesn't satisfy strict `TFunction`). The MAINT-117 subagent flagged this as pre-existing, and the orchestrator applied a documented `as unknown as TFunction` cast — minimal change, contained to one line.
- **TEST-45 was already closed; the subagent correctly no-op'd.** The user's tier-1 list said "TEST-42/45". The TEST-45 subagent ran, found the regression test was already in `oauth.rs`, and made no changes (file is unmodified in `git status`). This is the right behavior — but a more efficient process would have had the orchestrator pre-check item state before dispatch. Logged as a minor process note.
- **H-14 deviation from spec was reviewed carefully.** The bulk-SELECT-before-txn pattern is more efficient than per-op-in-txn (constant write-lock window vs proportional-to-batch-size) and TOCTOU-safe for Phase 1. The subagent reported the deviation upfront with the SQLITE_BUSY failure-mode evidence; the reviewer confirmed correctness via control-flow analysis. This is exactly the right pattern for handling subagent-side deviations: report up, review carefully, accept if sound.
- **Frontend test suite scaling.** Full vitest run took 92 seconds (8480 tests across 317 files). At this scale, subagents that need to run frontend tests should use targeted `-t '<pattern>'` or per-file invocations rather than full-suite runs. The orchestrator runs the full suite once at the end as the regression check.

- **Six parallel subagents on non-overlapping files, no worktrees, no stalls.** Same shape as the previous two sessions. End-to-end wall clock dominated by the longest build (MAINT-138 — full GCal API rewrite touched 6 methods).

The subagent reported the conflict with three resolution options instead of forcing the issue. Orchestrator picked the cleanest option (strip the duplicate body comment, keep the function as a contract marker) and applied it directly — 1-line edit, ran the affected tests, done.
- **Orchestrator sqlx-cache regen pattern works.** Subagents are told NOT to run `cargo sqlx prepare` themselves. After all subagents settle, the orchestrator runs `cargo sqlx prepare --check -- --tests` once. This session it returned clean — none of the 6 changes touched compile-time SQL macros (the MAINT-138 helpers and the MAINT-145 doc-only changes use runtime `sqlx::query` or no SQL at all).
- **Intra-session deflake commit was a good idea.** Sessions 508 + 509 each flagged `mcp::server::tests::shutdown_during_active_connection_blocks_new_connects` and `sync_cert::tests::concurrent_get_or_create_returns_same_cert` as flaky (each had to retry to pass). This session opened with a deflake commit (`eb8c89f`: 100×10ms cert-test retry budget, 5s mcp deadlines, explicit socket-file removal) before starting the new batch. Subsequent full-suite runs in THIS session showed 2991 passed with no flaky retries. Worth doing immediately when flaky markers appear, since otherwise they accumulate and erode trust in the test suite.

**Reviewers:** Six review subagents, one per build cluster — all SHIP'd. Highlights:
- The MAINT-143 reviewer identified the UX-250 bug fix as desirable (matched FEATURE-MAP.md line 193) rather than a regression; pre-existing `block_tags`-only backlink behaviour was the bug.
- The MAINT-144 reviewer cross-checked the helper SQL against a sibling query in `commands/blocks/crud.rs:125-126` (which uses hardcoded `i64::MAX` instead of the named `NULL_POSITION_SENTINEL`); the helper's parameterised binding is more maintainable.
- The TEST-51 reviewer mathematically verified the 4096-char digest fixture (42 × 95 + 64 + 42 = 4096) and confirmed the `<=` cap-inclusive convention in `format_description_full`.

**Process notes:**

- **Six parallel subagents on non-overlapping files, no worktrees.** Same shape as Session 508; this batch had no stalls. End-to-end wall clock dominated by the longest build (TEST-50B with 6 tests across 4 files).
- **Subagents observed each other's working-tree state but did NOT step on each other.** TEST-51 noted snapshot/tests.rs was being concurrently edited by TEST-50B and a transient compile-error window; TEST-51 used a saved backup workaround for its test run, then reverted. MAINT-143 noted `.sqlx/` deltas from the TEST-50B snapshot work and reverted them too. Final orchestrator-level `cargo sqlx prepare` regenerated the cache cleanly. No conflicts.
- **The orchestrator regenerates the sqlx cache once after all subagents settle.** Subagents that touch SQL should NOT run `cargo sqlx prepare` themselves (it would conflict with siblings); the orchestrator does it after the final `cargo nextest run` clean. This pattern worked well this session.
- **One commit, one prek run.** All 21 file changes ship in a single commit so the `prek run --all-files` hook covers the whole batch.
- **Incidental bug fix bonus.** MAINT-143's dedup wasn't expected to change behaviour — it just looked like a refactor. The reviewer flagged that the old `backlink/filters.rs` query was missing the inline-ref UNION (a pre-existing UX-250 contract violation) and the new helper-routed path correctly unions both. Net: REVIEW-LATER says "MAINT-143 done", and the BUG side ledger gets one more silent regression closed. This is a strong argument for dedup-style refactors when one side already has a documented invariant the other side is missing.

**Process notes:**

- **Six-parallel subagent dispatch with non-overlapping files.** Each test file was owned by exactly one subagent so no merge-conflict risk. No worktrees needed.
- **Subagent stall handling.** TEST-46's subagent ran for ~10 min without producing output or modifying the target file; killed the shell and the orchestrator did the fix directly (~5 lines). The fallback worked because the file was already pre-read for review-readiness, so the orchestrator had full context.
- **Subagent-driven REVIEW-LATER.md edits are race-prone.** The TEST-44 subagent updated REVIEW-LATER.md inline (removed its row + detail section + bumped the summary count from 94 → 93) before the others were done. The other 7 closures were rolled up by the orchestrator at the end. For future batches: subagents should NOT edit REVIEW-LATER.md; the orchestrator handles all REVIEW-LATER edits in one pass after every subagent reports back. (The TEST-44 subagent's edits were correct in content, but the workflow risks two subagents racing to bump the same `93 → 92` line with stale snapshots.)
- **Pipelined review while builds finish.** As each build subagent reported, its review subagent launched in parallel — at peak there were 3 active builds + 3 active reviews running concurrently (6 total). End-to-end wall clock was bounded by the longest build (TEST-46's stall) rather than the sum.
- **One commit, one prek run.** All 10 file changes ship in a single commit so the `prek run --all-files` hook covers the whole batch.

## Session 507 — Architecture audit → MAINT-112 CommandTx end-to-end + H-5/H-6 close + clippy-1.95 sweep (2026-04-27)

**3 items closed (MAINT-112, H-5, H-6), 3 items filed (MAINT-112/113/114), 19 pre-existing clippy errors swept.** Long-arc session driven by an adversarial architecture audit that tested "is the architecture fit for purpose?" against four parallel explorer subagents + two reviewer subagents. The explorers produced the usual exaggerations — a claimed "70:1 LOC of sync per 1 LOC of commands" that was actually ~0.78:1, a "velocity-negative momentum" framing that the data refuted, a "ConflictFreeBlockId newtype fixes everything" recommendation the reviewer rescoped to "do not do on a deadline". The reviewers' pass refuted every dramatic recommendation; the verdict landed on **SHIP — architecture is fit for purpose, do not revisit any earlier decision, do not refactor significantly**. Three opportunistic-cleanup items came out of that audit and were filed: MAINT-112 (CommandTx), MAINT-113 (ConflictFreeBlockId, deferred indefinitely), MAINT-114 (workflows consolidation, spike first). MAINT-112 was then executed end-to-end in two phases, the pre-existing clippy-1.95 regression in the tree was swept, and the remaining two HIGH-severity materializer items (H-5, H-6) were closed — with a fix that rejected the pass-2 recommendations in favour of a simpler "remove the JoinSet entirely" redesign.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**

- **Top-level:** 95 open items → 94 (MAINT-112 closed, MAINT-113 + MAINT-114 filed, net change −1 since the audit also filed those three in a prior commit within this session's window). Summary text: `58 planned work` → `57`.
- **Backend Code Review appendix — HIGH findings:** `(6)` → `(4)` after H-5 + H-6 deleted.
- **Stray cross-references cleaned:** MAINT-112's detail block had incorrectly cited "session 495 closed H-5 and H-6" (those items were still open until this session, and they describe JoinSet parallelism in the consumer, not the missing-dispatch failure class in the command layer — MAINT-112's actual concern). Reworded the two misattributions + removed a dangling "cf. H-3 / H-4 / H-5" reference in L-133 (only H-3 actually describes the unscoped-pages-after-bootstrap mechanism).

Python scripts handle the mechanical `&mut *tx` → `&mut **tx` rewrite (scoped to each migrated function's line range) and the 13 test-site materializer injections. Specta bindings regenerated cleanly (trailing-whitespace diff only; no public IPC signature changes). | Commit 4. Reviewer subagent: SHIP, high confidence, all 15 rubric items AFFIRM. |
| 6. H-5 / H-6 | The previous reviewer had not-quite-pushed on two HIGH items that MAINT-112's problem statement touched adjacent-to (the `dispatch_background_or_warn`-after-commit framing). On direct investigation: they describe a DIFFERENT bug class — the foreground consumer's `JoinSet` parallelism, bucketed by `extract_block_id(task)`, that could race parent/child CreateBlock ops (H-5) or could run a batch in parallel with an independent `ApplyOp` for one of the batch's non-first block_ids (H-6). The pass-2 recommendations in REVIEW-LATER called for a "root-of-op" grouping key (H-5) or multi-key overlap detection (H-6). Both were rejected in favour of a simpler fix: **delete the JoinSet, run foreground tasks strictly FIFO.** SQLite serialises writes at the engine level (WAL, single writer), so the JoinSet path bought no throughput. | Commit 5. Reviewer subagent: SHIP, high confidence, all 14 rubric items AFFIRM (one UNVERIFIABLE on historical MAINT-46 observability, resolved as "no regression"). |

**`CommandTx` surface (final shape, after phases A + B):**

```rust
// src/db.rs
pub struct CommandTx { /* inner: Transaction<'static>; pending: Vec<PendingDispatch>; label */ }
impl CommandTx {
    pub async fn begin_immediate(&SqlitePool, label: &'static str) -> Result<Self, sqlx::Error>;
    pub fn enqueue_background(&mut self, OpRecord);
    pub fn enqueue_edit_background(&mut self, OpRecord, impl Into<String>);
    pub async fn commit_and_dispatch(self, &Materializer) -> Result<usize, sqlx::Error>;
    pub async fn commit_without_dispatch(self) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error>;
    pub async fn rollback(self) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error>;
    pub fn pending_len(&self) -> usize;
}
impl Deref<Target = Transaction<'static, Sqlite>>;
impl DerefMut<Target = Transaction<'static, Sqlite>>;
```

**Reviews:**

- Audit phase: 2 reviewer subagents (one on backend/sync audit, one on frontend + tech-debt audit). Key refutations: "70:1 sync-to-commands LOC" was actually ~0.78:1 (the explorer had included test files in the sync side); "velocity-negative momentum" was refuted by the data (sessions 489–498 closed 105 items with 4 new files); "abandon roving editor" directly contradicts the documented docs/ARCHITECTURE.md constraint.
- Phase A reviewer: SHIP, high confidence, 10/10 rubric items AFFIRM. One doc precision nit (explicit `&mut *cmd_tx` vs. coerced `&mut cmd_tx`) applied in-place.
- Phase B reviewer: SHIP, high confidence, 15/15 rubric items AFFIRM.
- H-5 / H-6 reviewer: SHIP, high confidence, 14/14 rubric items AFFIRM (one UNVERIFIABLE on MAINT-46 historical observability — resolved as "no regression": the malformed-payload warn that previously lived inside the deleted `extract_block_id` now surfaces inside `apply_op_tx`'s `serde_json` parse path, same observability).

**Process notes:**

- **Adversarial review works.** Every high-confidence exploratory claim was tested against the code; ~1/3 were refuted or significantly downgraded (the "70:1" figure, the "velocity-negative" framing, the "abandon roving editor" recommendation). The architecture-is-fit-for-purpose verdict held up under two rounds of pressure. **Rule of thumb emerging:** explorer-only reviews are dramatic by bias; a paired reviewer subagent is cheap insurance.
- **"Delete it" as a fix strategy beat both pass-2 recommendations for H-5 / H-6.** Pass-2 proposed "root-of-op" grouping (H-5) or multi-key overlap detection (H-6); both were more elaborate than necessary. The right question was "does SQLite's WAL single-writer semantics let the JoinSet dispatch do useful work?" — answer no, so the correct simplification was "delete the JoinSet". The reviewer subagent explicitly affirmed that the simpler fix was superior to both pass-2 proposals.
- **Python scripts for mechanical rewrites pay off at scale.** The phase B migration included ~115 `&mut *tx` → `&mut **tx` rewrites scoped to specific function bodies, plus ~17 `create_page_in_space_inner(...)` test-callsite rewrites to inject `&materializer` as the 3rd argument, plus ~13 `let materializer = Materializer::new(pool.clone())` injections at the top of affected test functions. Writing one Python script per rewrite class and scoping each to a function line-range was faster and safer than manual editing across 15 files.
- **Pre-existing clippy drift is a tree-level liability.** The clippy-1.95 sweep pre-empted the prek `cargo-clippy` hook: without that sweep, every subsequent commit would have failed the hook on files the commit didn't touch. Landing the sweep as its own commit (separate from MAINT-112 phase B) made both commits cleaner to review and leaves a cleaner audit trail.
- **Specta bindings regenerate cleanly when only `_inner` signatures change.** Phase B touched 11 `_inner` functions but no Tauri command signatures. The regenerated `src/lib/bindings.ts` diff was trailing-whitespace-only. This is the expected behaviour — the `tauri-specta` pipeline reflects only the `#[tauri::command]` surface, not inner helpers.
- **5 commits in one session is on the high end.** Each commit had its own reviewer pass, so the review budget was ~4 subagent rounds including the initial audit. In a MORE-focused session I would have merged phases A + B into one commit and folded the clippy sweep into "phase A compile-prep". The separation was deliberate here so the audit-prompted filing could stand alone and so the clippy sweep could land without mixing MAINT-112 concerns.

- `src-tauri/src/mcp/last_append.rs`, `mcp/tools_ro.rs`, `mcp/tools_rw.rs`

## Session 506 — Core/materializer/compaction sweep: 4 BCR items (M-1 + M-2 + M-10 + L-42) (2026-04-27)

**4 items closed.** A non-sync defensive-coding batch spanning Core (M-1 ULID normalization on `attachment_id`), Materializer (M-10 refcount-bump clone for `BatchApplyOps`), Commands (System) (L-42 real `compact_op_log` deleted count), and a doc-accuracy fix (M-2 — docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §15 names the actual sanitization mechanism). 3 parallel build subagents + 1 orchestrator-direct doc update; all 4 first-pass cleanly with no review-required fixes.

**REVIEW-LATER impact (BCR appendix):** Medium count 32 → 28. Domain table: Core data layer Medium 6 → 4 (M-1 + M-2); Materializer Medium 6 → 5 (M-10); Commands (System) Medium 11 → 10 (L-42).

**Batch composition:**

| ID | Files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| **M-1** (S — ULID norm) | `src-tauri/src/op.rs`, `src-tauri/src/op_log.rs`, `src-tauri/src/commands/attachments.rs`, `src-tauri/src/commands/history.rs`, `src-tauri/src/materializer/handlers.rs`, `src-tauri/src/reverse/attachment_ops.rs`, + 7 test files | `AddAttachmentPayload.attachment_id` and `DeleteAttachmentPayload.attachment_id` migrated from `String` to the existing `AttachmentId` alias of `BlockId`. `BlockId` is `#[serde(transparent)]` so the wire format is byte-identical to what shipped before, but feeding `serialize_inner_payload` → `compute_op_hash` now goes through the normalization contract required by AGENTS.md invariant #8. Without this, a frontend bug stamping a lowercase attachment_id on one device would produce a different op hash on a peer device and the hash chain would silently fail to converge — a single-user multi-device sync correctness bug. 2 production callers construct payloads via `BlockId::from_trusted(&attachment_id)`; 3 sqlx `.bind(&p.attachment_id)` sites migrated to `.bind(p.attachment_id.as_str())` (matches the canonical convention for `BlockId` binds). 2 existing op-payload snapshot files (`op_payload_json_add_attachment.snap`, `op_payload_json_delete_attachment.snap`) untouched — proof the wire format didn't move. **Test:** `attachment_id_normalization_lowercase_and_uppercase_produce_same_hash_m1` in `op_log.rs` builds two equivalent JSON payloads differing only in case, deserializes both, and asserts identical hashes. |
| **M-10** (S — refcount clone) | `src-tauri/src/materializer/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/materializer/consumer.rs`, `src-tauri/src/materializer/handlers.rs`, `src-tauri/src/materializer/dedup.rs`, `src-tauri/src/materializer/tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_protocol/operations.rs` | `MaterializeTask::BatchApplyOps(Vec<OpRecord>)` → `BatchApplyOps(Arc<Vec<OpRecord>>)`. The 3 `task.clone()` sites in `materializer/consumer.rs` (pre-first-attempt prep + two retry-dispatch sites in `run_background`) now bump a refcount instead of deep-cloning a multi-thousand-op Vec on every task. Matters most on the happy path (first attempt succeeds): previously the clone was pure waste; now it's an atomic fetch-add. Mobile (Android) RAM footprint shrinks correspondingly. Consumer pattern-match sites already use `records.first()` / `records.iter()` which compile unchanged through `Arc<Vec<T>>`'s deref — the only functional change was `handlers.rs:42` where `for record in records` had to become `for record in records.iter()` (`&Arc<Vec<T>>` isn't `IntoIterator`). 1 production construction site (`sync_protocol/operations.rs:168`) + 6 test fixtures wrapped in `Arc::new(...)`. **Test:** `batch_apply_ops_no_retry_does_not_deep_clone_m10` asserts `Arc::strong_count` semantics and `Arc::ptr_eq` identity across a task clone. |
| **L-42** (S — real compact count) | `src-tauri/src/snapshot/create.rs`, `src-tauri/src/commands/compaction.rs`, `src-tauri/src/snapshot/tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/compaction_cmd_tests.rs` | Pre-fix `compact_op_log_cmd_inner` reported `ops_deleted = eligible_in_tx` — the count eligible at the start of the pre-flight tx, NOT the count actually deleted by the subsequent `snapshot::compact_op_log`. Between pre-flight commit and inner delete, more ops could be appended (their `created_at` may still be `< cutoff` if the wall clock advanced) and the snapshot-frontier guard inside `compact_op_log` could skip some. `snapshot::compact_op_log` return type changed from `Result<Option<String>, AppError>` to `Result<Option<(String, u64)>, AppError>`; the wrapper destructures the tuple and populates `CompactionResult.ops_deleted` with the real count. `CompactionResult` wire shape unchanged (`ops_deleted: i64` stays — the new `u64` is cast). No `src/lib/bindings.ts` regen. 1 production caller updated to destructure the tuple; 4 test discard sites (`.is_some()`, `.unwrap()` without field access) unchanged. **Test:** `compact_op_log_returns_real_deleted_count_l42` asserts `result.ops_deleted == (COUNT before - COUNT after)` against the actual op_log. |
| **M-2** (S — doc accuracy, orchestrator-direct) | `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` | The BCR's M-2 framing was a false positive: `sanitize_internal_error` at `src-tauri/src/commands/mod.rs` IS applied at 89 of 102 Tauri command wrappers via `.map_err(sanitize_internal_error)`, collapsing `Database` / `Migration` / `Io` / `Json` / `Channel` / `Snapshot` variants to `AppError::InvalidOperation("an internal error occurred")` and emitting a `tracing::warn!` with the original. The doc's promise in §15 was upheld — it just happens at the command-wrapper `map_err`, not in `AppError`'s `Serialize` impl as the BCR assumed. Doc now names the actual mechanism: cite file/function, enumerate which variants sanitize vs pass-through, note that adding the wrapper is the contract for any new Tauri command. L-48 tracks the drift on the 13 commands that skip the wrapper — separate item, not conflated into M-2. Orchestrator-direct (no subagent needed) because the fix is a single paragraph edit. |

**Reviews:**

- R1 (technical, 21 dimensions, foreground): **PASS** with no fixes needed. All 4 items verified individually. Cross-cutting: `cargo build --tests` clean, `cargo nextest run op_log op materializer compact` shows 868 tests pass. UX reviewer correctly NOT launched — 3 code changes are backend-internal with no user-visible surface; 1 is doc-only.

**Process notes:**

- **Verifying BCR descriptions against the codebase catches false positives.** M-2's BCR description said the `AppError::Serialize` impl forwards raw messages to the frontend. Technically true at the Serialize layer, but irrelevant — 89 of 102 command wrappers transform the error via `sanitize_internal_error` BEFORE it hits Serialize, so the user-facing error IS already generic. Fixing M-2 as "change Serialize impl to sanitize" (BCR's option a) would have re-sanitized already-sanitized errors AND broken the 13 commands that rely on richer error surfaces. Orchestrator-direct reading of `commands/mod.rs` before launching a subagent revealed this — the correct fix is the doc update (option b), naming the real mechanism. **Lesson:** when a BCR description sounds like "the code doesn't do X, but the doc says it does," spend 5 minutes verifying the code's actual behavior across adjacent layers before launching a subagent to "fix" it.
- **3 parallel subagents + 1 orchestrator-direct = optimal parallelism.** The M-2 doc fix could have been a 4th subagent (with paragraph-level instructions), but reading the BCR + skimming `commands/mod.rs` is faster than spinning up a subagent context. Applying it orchestrator-direct while the 3 subagents ran returned better end-to-end time. **Rule emerging:** if a fix is a paragraph edit AND the orchestrator has already read the relevant file, do it orchestrator-direct.
- **Single-batch reviewer continues to work.** 21 dimensions across 4 items in one foreground review slot. This is now the default for defensive-coding batches regardless of domain.
- **Cross-subagent file-naming coincidence.** M-1 and M-10 both touched `materializer/handlers.rs` (M-1 for sqlx bind updates, M-10 for the `.iter()` fix). The subagents didn't conflict because they modified different lines. The M-10 subagent noted the M-1 build errors during its initial `cargo build --tests` and re-ran after a `cargo clean` once M-1's writes landed. No orchestrator intervention needed.

**Files touched (21 code + 1 doc):** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (M-2 §15 paragraph); `src-tauri/src/op.rs` (M-1 struct types); `src-tauri/src/op_log.rs` (M-1 test + test-helper); `src-tauri/src/commands/attachments.rs` (M-1 production callers); `src-tauri/src/commands/history.rs` (M-1 bind); `src-tauri/src/materializer/handlers.rs` (M-1 bind + M-10 iter); `src-tauri/src/reverse/attachment_ops.rs` (M-1 bind); `src-tauri/src/reverse/tests.rs` (M-1 fixtures); `src-tauri/src/recovery/tests.rs` (M-1 fixtures); `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/block_cmd_tests.rs` (M-1 fixtures); `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/undo_redo_tests.rs` (M-1 fixtures); `src-tauri/src/gcal_push/dirty_producer.rs` (M-1 fixture); `src-tauri/src/materializer/mod.rs` (M-10 variant); `src-tauri/src/materializer/consumer.rs` (M-10 test module); `src-tauri/src/materializer/dedup.rs` (M-10 test fixture); `src-tauri/src/materializer/tests.rs` (M-10 fixtures); `src-tauri/src/sync_protocol/operations.rs` (M-10 production); `src-tauri/src/snapshot/create.rs` (L-42 return type); `src-tauri/src/snapshot/tests.rs` (L-42 caller); `src-tauri/src/commands/compaction.rs` (L-42 wrapper); `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/compaction_cmd_tests.rs` (L-42 test).

**Commits (2):** `fix(core): close 4 BCR items (M-1, M-2, M-10, L-42)` (21 files, 424+ / 67−), `docs(session-506): close 4 BCR items + log session` (REVIEW-LATER pruning + this entry).

## Session 505 — Sync cancellation + clarity sweep: 4 BCR Medium items (M-33 + M-46 + M-47 + M-48) (2026-04-27)

**4 items closed.** A second sync defensive-coding batch focused on cancellation propagation (M-46 daemon-loop scope, M-47 file-transfer phase) plus a truncation-detection improvement (M-48) and a comment-only clarity fix (M-33). All four shipped via 3 parallel build subagents with no orchestrator-direct fallbacks needed this time. The session-504 abandonment of M-46 (CancelGuard semantics) was un-blocked here by writing a tightly-locked-in subagent prompt that picked the "return bool from try_sync_with_peer" approach explicitly, removing the option (a)/(b) ambiguity that stalled the previous run.

**REVIEW-LATER impact (BCR appendix):** Medium count 36 → 32 (4 closed). Sync stack row in domain table: 13 → 10. Commands (System) row: 12 → 11. The 4 BCR HIGH items + 6 remaining HIGH families are unchanged.

**Batch composition:**

| ID | Files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| **M-33** (S — clarity) | `src-tauri/src/commands/sync_cmds.rs` | `start_sync_inner`'s `_guard` is renamed to `_health_check_only_guard` and gets a multi-line doc-comment explaining the wrapper's `try_lock_peer` is a probe ("is the daemon currently syncing this peer?") — NOT a real exclusion lock. The guard falls out of scope when `start_sync_inner` returns microseconds later; real per-peer serialisation lives in `sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs::try_sync_with_peer` where the daemon's own `try_lock_peer` holds the lock for the duration of the actual network sync. The comment names that file/path explicitly so the next reader can find the truth without re-deriving it. Comment-only behavioural change; no tests added (no existing test asserted the unreachable "Sync already in progress" error path). |
| **M-46** (S — cancel scope) | `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/tests.rs` | `try_sync_with_peer` return type changed from `()` to `bool`: `true` iff the cancel flag was observed when the session ended, `false` for early-exit paths (backoff, lock contention, no addresses, connect failure — they didn't run a real session). The capture happens AFTER `run_sync_session` returns but BEFORE `_cancel_guard`'s `Drop::drop` fires (the guard is the first local declared so it drops last in Rust's reverse-declaration order — verified by reading the function). The daemon's Branch B (`for peer_ref in &refs`) and Branch C (`for pid in due`) capture the bool and `break` if true; Branch A (single mDNS-discovery call) discards via `let _cancelled = ...`. Four early-exit `return;` statements inside `try_sync_with_peer` were converted to `return false;`. **Tests (4):** `try_sync_with_peer_returns_true_when_cancel_observed_during_session_m46` (asserts the early-exit `false` return when the network can't be mocked), `try_sync_with_peer_returns_false_on_backoff_early_exit_m46`, `daemon_loop_breaks_round_when_cancel_observed_during_first_peer_m46` (replicates the exact `for peer_ref in &refs { let cancelled = stub(...); if cancelled { break; } }` pattern with a stub that returns true for PEER_1; asserts only PEER_1 is visited), and `daemon_loop_visits_all_peers_when_no_cancel_observed_m46` (regression guard for the no-cancel case). |
| **M-47** (M — file-transfer cancel) | `src-tauri/src/sync_files.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/server.rs` | `cancel: &AtomicBool` parameter threaded through 4 functions: `run_file_transfer_initiator`, `run_file_transfer_responder`, and the inner helpers `request_and_receive_files` + `receive_request_and_send_files` (the cancel checks live inside the inner helpers, so the parameter has to thread down). Cancel checks at per-file loop boundaries — sender at the top of the `for attachment_id in &attachment_ids` loop (falls through to the existing `FileTransferComplete` send), receiver at the top of its `loop { ... }` before `recv_json()`. **No new wire variants** — the existing `FileTransferComplete` "I'm done with files" message is reused as the cancel signal so the protocol stays backward-compatible. Production caller `run_sync_session` in the orchestrator passes its own `cancel` through (line 729). Server-side responder caller (`sync_daemon/server.rs:310`) constructs a fresh `AtomicBool::new(false)` with an inline comment explicitly noting that responder-side cancellation is a follow-up — the user-visible initiator path is the priority. 12 test call-sites of the inner helpers updated to pass `&AtomicBool::new(false)` (or a pair of `&cancel_resp` / `&cancel_init` locals where `tokio::join!` arms need two distinct refs for borrow-checker reasons). **Test:** `run_file_transfer_initiator_breaks_on_cancel_m47` — sets up two missing attachments, sets `cancel.store(true)` after file 1's bytes arrive, asserts file 1 is on disk + file 2 is NOT received + `stats.files_received == 1`. |
| **M-48** (S — truncation detection) | `src-tauri/src/sync_files.rs` | `find_missing_attachments` now compares `tokio::fs::metadata().len()` against `attachments.size_bytes` for every row. Size mismatch → re-classify as missing. Metadata errors (permission denied, race with delete, etc.) ALSO classify as missing — the defensive choice that matches AGENTS.md's "preventing accidental corruption" framing for backup/sync stacks. `tracing::warn!` cites M-48 with `attachment_id`, `path`, `expected_size`, and `actual_size` so support sessions have a breadcrumb. The function signature is unchanged (caller-compatible). The `attachments` schema is unchanged — no new `blake3_hash` column (that's the deferred "better path" the BCR also describes for content-level verification, requires user approval per "Architectural Stability"). Two existing happy-path tests had `size_bytes` adjusted from arbitrary `512` to the actual byte length of their fixture payloads (the prior tests never exercised the size comparison; arbitrary mismatched values were silently fine). **Test:** `find_missing_attachments_treats_truncated_file_as_missing_m48` — 0-byte file vs `size_bytes = 1024` is asserted to be in the missing list. |

**Reviews:**

- R1 (technical, 26 dimensions, foreground): **PASS** with no fixes needed. All 4 items verified individually plus cross-cutting build / test / AGENTS.md invariants. The reviewer ran `cargo nextest run sync_daemon sync_files sync_cmds` to spot-check. UX reviewer correctly NOT launched — every change is backend-internal with no user-visible surface beyond more accurate `tracing::warn!` lines and "cancel sync" actually stopping the round (which IS user-visible but doesn't change any layout, copy, or interaction).

**Process notes:**

- **Tightening the M-46 prompt rescued it from session 504's abandonment.** Session 504's M-46 subagent ran past the deadline because the BCR description offered options (a) and (b) without picking one, and the body of `try_sync_with_peer` is large enough that an agent without a locked-in approach can wander. This session's prompt explicitly: (1) chose "return bool from try_sync_with_peer" as THE approach, (2) listed every early-exit `return;` site to convert, (3) named the exact line where `was_cancelled = cancel.load(...)` should live (after the match block, before the `conn.close()` cleanup, before the implicit guard drop), and (4) listed every Branch (A/B/C) in the daemon loop with the exact pattern (`let cancelled = ...; if cancelled { break; }` for B/C, `let _cancelled =` for A). The subagent shipped first-pass with no orchestrator post-build fixes. **Lesson: when a previous session's subagent stalled on a multi-option BCR description, write the next prompt with the option chosen and every site enumerated.**
- **3 parallel subagents, all 3 returned cleanly first time.** Session 504 had 2 stuck builds out of 5; this session had 0 out of 3. The narrower scope per subagent (each subagent owns 1-2 closely-related items in distinct files: M-33 alone in `sync_cmds.rs`; M-46+M-47 sharing `orchestrator.rs` + `sync_files.rs`; M-48 alone in `sync_files.rs`'s `find_missing_attachments`) plus the lock-in pattern from M-46 made the difference. Cross-subagent file conflict on `sync_files.rs` between M-47 and M-48 was bounded by explicit "M-47 owns `run_file_transfer_*`, M-48 owns `find_missing_attachments`, do not touch the other's function" instructions — no merge conflict at the orchestrator level.
- **Single batch reviewer pattern continues to work.** One technical reviewer with a 26-dimension matrix replaced 4 individual reviewers. Per-item PASS/FAIL verdicts plus cross-cutting verification all in one slot. Worth using as the default for backend defensive-coding batches.

**Files touched (5):** `src-tauri/src/commands/sync_cmds.rs` (M-33 rename + comment), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs` (M-46 bool return + Branches B/C break + M-47 caller passes `cancel`), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/server.rs` (M-47 responder fresh-flag with deferred-trade-off comment), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/tests.rs` (4 new M-46 tests + 3 caller updates), `src-tauri/src/sync_files.rs` (M-47 `cancel` thread through 4 functions + 12 caller updates + 1 new test; M-48 size check + 2 existing-test adjustments + 1 new test).

**Commits (2):** `fix(sync): close 4 BCR Medium items (M-33, M-46, M-47, M-48)` (5 files, 690+ / 43−), `docs(session-505): close 4 BCR Medium items + log session` (REVIEW-LATER pruning + this entry).

## Session 504 — Sync defensive-coding sweep: 5 BCR Medium items (M-54 + M-56 + M-57 + M-58 + M-66) (2026-04-27)

**5 items closed.** A defensive-coding batch across the sync stack (cert torn-write recovery, TLS device-id binding, OnceLock migration, snapshot frontier check) and the FEAT-6 RESET path (drafts-warn). All five shipped as a single feature commit — different files, no new wire messages, no schema migrations, no architectural changes. **M-46** (cancel-flag scope between peers) was attempted but its build subagent ran past the deadline and was abandoned per the session-501 precedent — the fix needs more space than a parallel slot allowed since it requires moving `CancelGuard` from per-session to per-round semantics, a non-trivial control-flow change.

**REVIEW-LATER impact (BCR appendix):** Medium count 41 → 36 (5 closed). Sync stack row in domain table: 17 → 13 Medium. Lifecycle / Snapshots row: 18 → 17 Medium. The 4 BCR HIGH items + 6 remaining HIGH families are unchanged.

**Batch composition:**

| ID | Files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| **M-54** (S — sync_cert) | `src-tauri/src/sync_cert.rs` | `get_or_create_sync_cert` now detects an orphaned `.pem` (no matching `.hash` — the torn-write signature of a crash between the two `sync_all`s) at the top of the create path, warns, and deletes the stale `.pem` so the regen path runs cleanly. A 500 ms staleness gate (`M54_TORN_WRITE_STALENESS`) disambiguates a real torn write (post-crash, `.pem` is at minimum seconds old) from the deliberately concurrent startup race the existing `concurrent_get_or_create_returns_same_cert` test exercises (sub-millisecond gap between a winner thread's two `sync_all`s) — without the gate, a retrying loser thread would actively *delete* the winner's mid-write `.pem` and corrupt state. New tests: `get_or_create_sync_cert_recovers_from_torn_write_m54` (happy recovery — sleeps 600 ms to age past threshold) + `get_or_create_sync_cert_does_not_recover_fresh_orphan_m54` (anti-regression — fresh orphan returns `AppError::InvalidOperation`, `.pem` not deleted). |
| **M-56 + M-57** (S — TLS verifier) | `src-tauri/src/sync_net/tls.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_net/connection.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_net/tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs` (1-line caller update), `src-tauri/src/sync_files.rs` + `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/tests.rs` (test caller updates) | **M-56:** `PinningCertVerifier` gains optional `expected_remote_id: Option<String>`; when `Some(eid)` the verifier rejects certs whose CN doesn't match `agaric-{eid}` exactly. TOFU semantics preserved — callers without a known peer id (first-pair, tests) pass `None` and the existing `agaric-*` prefix check still applies. Production caller `try_connect_each_address` now passes `Some(peer_id)`; 19 test callsites updated to pass `None`. **M-57:** `Arc<std::sync::Mutex<Option<String>>>` → `Arc<OnceLock<String>>` for `observed_hash` shared between `connect_to_peer` and the verifier. Verifier writes via `set(...).ok()` (idempotent on chain — only the leaf is recorded). Caller reads via `get().cloned()`. No more `.lock().unwrap()`, no more poisoning. New tests: `m56_verifier_rejects_cn_for_unexpected_device_id`, `m56_verifier_accepts_cn_for_matching_device_id`, `m57_observed_hash_is_set_once_and_survives_repeated_verify`. |
| **M-58** (S — snapshot frontier) | `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/snapshot_transfer.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/server.rs`, `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/tests.rs` | Before sending `SnapshotOffer`, `try_offer_snapshot_catchup` now compares the snapshot's `up_to_seqs` against the remote's heads from the orchestrator session via the new private helper `snapshot_covers_remote_heads(snapshot_seqs, remote_heads) -> Result<(), String>`. On mismatch sends `SyncMessage::Error` (existing wire variant — no new message), returns the new `OfferOutcome::SnapshotStale { reason }`, and emits `SyncEvent::Error`. `sync_daemon/server.rs` now captures `remote_heads: Vec<DeviceHead>` from the first incoming `HeadExchange` BEFORE the orchestrator consumes it, then threads the slice through. Closes the silent failure mode where compaction-vs-retention drift could leave the responder happily offering a snapshot that doesn't cover the remote's progress, only to have the receiver re-apply an older snapshot and silently lose data. Existing `feat6_end_to_end_compact_then_snapshot_catchup` integration test was updated (the prior synthetic test was actually exercising the M-58 silent-data-loss scenario; a comment now explains why an additional FEAT6_INIT op is seeded on the responder before snapshotting). New tests: `snapshot_covers_remote_heads_truth_table` (sync, no I/O) covers empty/ahead/equal/behind/missing-device + asserts the error string names the offending device and both seqs; `try_offer_snapshot_catchup_sends_error_when_snapshot_behind_remote` is the wire-level scenario. |
| **M-66** (S — apply_snapshot drafts warn — orchestrator-direct) | `src-tauri/src/snapshot/restore.rs`, `src-tauri/src/snapshot/tests.rs` | `apply_snapshot` now reads `COUNT(*) FROM block_drafts` + samples up to 8 `block_id`s inside the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` wipe transaction BEFORE the existing `DELETE FROM block_drafts`, and emits a structured `tracing::warn!` with `dropped_count` + `sample_ids` when the count is > 0. The 8-row sample bounds log size on a pathological peer with hundreds of unflushed drafts; the count itself is unbounded. Surfaces drafts silently lost during FEAT-6 RESET — pre-fix this happened with no log line, no count returned, and no test asserting the drop, making "where did my typing go?" a true mystery to debug. **Applied orchestrator-direct** because the M-66 build subagent ran past its deadline (same hang pattern as M-46 and as session-501's FEAT-3p5b) — the change is small enough (~25 LOC + 1 regression test) that orchestrator-direct + immediate `cargo nextest run apply_snapshot` (9/9 pass) is faster than re-launching another subagent. New test `apply_snapshot_drops_drafts_observably_m66` seeds 3 drafts, calls `apply_snapshot`, asserts post-state empty; the comment documents that a regression re-ordering DELETE before COUNT would always count zero and silently re-introduce the bug. |

**Reviews:**

- R1 (technical, 20 dimensions, foreground): **PASS** with no fixes needed. All 5 items verified individually. Cross-cutting: `cargo build --tests` clean, `cargo nextest run sync_ snapshot` shows 526 tests pass (one flaky recovered on retry), AGENTS.md invariants preserved. UX reviewer correctly NOT launched — every change is backend-internal with no user-visible surface beyond more accurate `tracing::warn!` lines.

**Process notes:**

- **Two stuck build subagents (M-46 + M-66). One abandoned + ported to next session, one orchestrator-direct.** This is the second occurrence of the runaway-build-subagent pattern (first was FEAT-3p5b in sessions 500-501). The asymmetry: M-66 was 25 LOC + 1 test → orchestrator-direct cleanly closed it inside the same session. M-46 was a non-trivial control-flow change (`CancelGuard` scope) → safer to file as remaining open. The rule emerging from sessions 501-504: **if a stuck subagent's task is truly S-cost and the orchestrator can write the change in one focused pass, do it directly; if it's M+ or requires distinct mental models, defer to next session**. Session 503's "tight prompt → first-pass PASS" pattern works for well-scoped changes (H-8); it doesn't always work for changes that touch invariants the BCR description glosses over (M-46 specifically conflicted with the existing per-session `CancelGuard` clear-on-drop semantic, and the spec's option (a)/(b) framing didn't make the trade-off explicit).
- **Cross-subagent file conflicts on `sync_daemon/tests.rs`.** Three subagents (M-56+M-57, M-58, and indirectly the M-46 subagent that didn't finish) all needed to update test callers in `sync_daemon/tests.rs`. The M-56+M-57 subagent reported it stashed-then-popped the parallel WIP to verify its own work; the M-58 subagent reported it had to update an existing FEAT-6 integration test that was incidentally exercising the M-58 silent-data-loss scenario. The orchestrator's final `cargo build --tests` pass plus `cargo nextest run sync_ snapshot` (526 passing) confirmed the merged tree is consistent. **Lesson:** when 3+ parallel subagents touch overlapping test files, the orchestrator should expect a small reconciliation pass after the last subagent merges (here it was already-clean by the time M-46 was abandoned).
- **Single batch reviewer instead of 5 individual reviewers.** Five small backend changes with no UX impact don't warrant 5 separate review subagents — one technical reviewer covering all 5 in a 20-dimension matrix gave the same coverage with one slot instead of five. Worth doing again for this shape of batch.

**Files touched (11):** `src-tauri/src/sync_cert.rs` (+~120 / -~5, M-54 detection + 2 tests + staleness const), `src-tauri/src/sync_net/tls.rs` (M-56 expected_remote_id + M-57 OnceLock), `src-tauri/src/sync_net/connection.rs` (M-56 plumbing + M-57 OnceLock read), `src-tauri/src/sync_net/tests.rs` (3 new tests + 15 caller updates + 3 ad-hoc PinningCertVerifier struct updates), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/orchestrator.rs` (1-line: `Some(peer_id)` for production caller), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/snapshot_transfer.rs` (M-58 helper + 2 tests + signature change), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/server.rs` (M-58 capture remote_heads from HeadExchange), `src-tauri/src/sync_daemon/tests.rs` (4 caller updates spanning M-56+M-57 + M-58), `src-tauri/src/sync_files.rs` (M-56+M-57 test helper update), `src-tauri/src/snapshot/restore.rs` (M-66 count + warn + comment block), `src-tauri/src/snapshot/tests.rs` (M-66 regression test).

**Commits (2):** `fix(sync): close 5 BCR Medium items (M-54, M-56, M-57, M-58, M-66)` (11 files, 834+ / 56−), `docs(session-504): close 5 BCR Medium items + log session` (REVIEW-LATER pruning + this entry).

## Session 503 — H-8 agenda cursor fix + H-3 cluster cleanup (2026-04-26)

**5 items closed.** One real backend bug fix (H-8 — `list_agenda_range` cursor encoding) plus four documentation-hygiene removals (H-3 cluster: parent + H-3a + H-3b + H-3c — all already shipped in earlier sessions but never cleaned out of REVIEW-LATER's BCR appendix). Net result: REVIEW-LATER's `HIGH findings` family count drops from 8 → 6, the Top-priority list loses H-3, the Quick wins table loses both H-3 and H-8, and the actual agenda pagination drift bug at `list_agenda_range` is fixed.

**REVIEW-LATER impact (BCR appendix):** HIGH families 8 → 6. Top-priority items 5 → 4 (renamed "Top-priority items" since the count drift is now baked-in policy as more items are closed). Quick wins table 4 → 2 rows (H-17, M-23 remain).

**Batch composition:**

| ID | Files | Change |
|---|---|---|
| **H-8** (S — backend, real fix) | `src-tauri/src/pagination/agenda.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pagination/tests.rs`, `.sqlx/` cache | `list_agenda_range`'s keyset cursor encoded a date derived from `b.due_date` / `b.scheduled_date` (BlockRow columns) into `Cursor::deleted_at`, but the SQL `WHERE` and `ORDER BY` filter on `ac.date` (the `agenda_cache.date` column). When an agenda entry's date came from a non-column source (e.g. `agenda_cache.source = 'property:custom_due'`), `ac.date != b.due_date` and the cursor drifted at page boundaries — silently skipping or duplicating entries between pages. **Fix:** refactor from `query_as!(BlockRow, …)` to raw `query!(…)` that additionally projects `ac.date as "ac_date: String"`. Build a parallel `Vec<String>` of agenda dates in lockstep with the `Vec<BlockRow>`, pre-trim both to `page.limit` so `ac_dates.last()` aligns with whatever row `build_page_response` keeps as the last row of the page. Capture `last_ac_date = ac_dates.last().cloned()` via `move` into the cursor closure. The bug-vector helper `extract_date_for_cursor` is removed entirely. `list_agenda` (single-date) is deliberately untouched — it doesn't use a composite `(date, block_id)` cursor. **Test:** new `list_agenda_range_cursor_uses_ac_date_not_block_dates_h8` regression — three blocks all with `b.due_date = '2025-01-01'` (the wrong date) + `agenda_cache.date = '2025-03-10'/'-11'/'-12'` with `source = 'property:custom_due'`. Page 1 limit=2 returns rows A+B with non-null `next_cursor`; the decoded cursor's `deleted_at == '2025-03-11'` (NOT `'2025-01-01'`). Page 2 with the cursor returns exactly C — no skip, no dupe. `pagination::tests` count: 104 → 105. `cargo sqlx prepare -- --tests` regenerated the cache. |
| **H-3** (parent), **H-3a**, **H-3b**, **H-3c** (cleanup — all shipped earlier) | `REVIEW-LATER.md` only | Verified all four entries are genuinely closed before removing: (a) H-3a — backend `create_block_inner_with_space` at `src-tauri/src/commands/blocks/crud.rs:259` rejects `block_type='page'` without `space_id` (returns `AppError::Validation`); the Tauri `create_block` IPC routes through it; tests `create_block_rejects_page_without_space_id` + `create_block_with_page_and_space_id_emits_two_ops_atomically` at `block_cmd_tests.rs:421-540` confirm the IPC contract. (b) H-3b — grep for `createBlock\(\{[^)]*blockType:\s*['"]page` across `src/` shows ZERO non-comment, non-test callsites — all 6 hits are migration comments (JournalPage, useBlockDatePicker, TemplatesView, tauri.ts) or assertion-of-non-use tests (tauri.test.ts, useBlockResolve.test.ts). (c) H-3c — `property_every_page_has_space_after_bootstrap_under_mixed_create_paths` test at `src-tauri/src/spaces/tests.rs:712` covers all three documented bypass paths (legacy IPC refusal, atomic create_page_in_space, sync replay backfill). The H-3 parent is just an umbrella — closing the three sub-items closes it. |

**Reviews:**

- R1 (technical, 14 dimensions, foreground): **PASS** with no fixes needed. Verified vector alignment between `Vec<BlockRow>` and `Vec<String>` (single-pass construction + synchronized trimming), `build_page_response` semantics (closure only invoked when `has_more = true`, after pre-trimming so `ac_dates.last()` matches the kept last row), edge cases (`ac.date` empty → impossible per schema NOT NULL + sqlx cache `nullable: false`; zero rows → closure not invoked, no panic; source filter still works). `list_agenda` (single-date) confirmed untouched. `extract_date_for_cursor` confirmed removed (zero hits anywhere). The `.sqlx/` cache regeneration was clean (one query JSON deleted, one added). No silent error swallowing, no `unsafe`, no relaxed `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`. UX reviewer was correctly NOT launched — backend-only cursor encoding fix has no user-facing surface.

**Process notes:**

- **Documentation lag is real and worth a session-half.** When PROMPT.md says "Group 3-6 related items into a batch", and the FEAT/PUB section of the summary table only has 4 actionable items left, looking deeper into the BCR appendix paid off — the H-3 cluster had been quietly shipped across sessions 497-498 (or earlier) without anyone removing the entries. Verifying-then-removing them in the same session as a real fix gave the session a clean "5 items closed" outcome instead of "1 fix, 4 stale entries left rotting". Future sessions should periodically grep for "shipped but not removed" — quick way to find these is to spot-check the open items' implementation files (`grep -n` for the symbol the entry references) before launching a build subagent.
- **The build subagent prompt was written tight enough to ship cleanly.** The H-8 fix had an obvious wrong path (rewriting `Cursor` struct, bumping a version field) and an obvious right path (use the existing `deleted_at` slot, just populate it correctly from `ac.date`). The prompt listed both and explicitly told the subagent which to pick. Returned PASS on first review with zero orchestrator post-build fixes — only one trivial `cargo fmt` line wrap that the orchestrator auto-fixed before commit.
- **No UX reviewer for backend-internal pagination changes.** PROMPT.md § 4 says "For backend-only changes, the UX reviewer is unnecessary." This was a textbook case — pure cursor-encoding bug, no IPC contract change, no user-visible surface beyond "agenda no longer skips/dupes at page boundaries" (which is itself only visible to power users with non-column-source agenda entries). Skipping the UX reviewer saved a parallel-subagent slot.

**Files touched (3 code, 2 docs):** `src-tauri/src/pagination/agenda.rs` (+44 / -22, removed `extract_date_for_cursor`, switched to `query!` + parallel ac_date Vec), `src-tauri/src/pagination/tests.rs` (+92 — single regression test), `src-tauri/.sqlx/` (1 query JSON deleted, 1 added — cache rotation), `REVIEW-LATER.md` (-52 lines: H-3 cluster + H-8 detail sections + Top-priority entry + Quick wins rows; -2 in HIGH count; section heading rename), `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry).

**Commits (2):** `fix(pagination): H-8 — list_agenda_range cursor encodes ac.date, not block columns` (3 files, 151+ / 22−), `docs(session-503): close H-8 + H-3 cluster cleanup + log session` (this entry + REVIEW-LATER pruning).
